The Short List

by "Ledger" Lin Marginal
◆ DATA-DERIVED
Ten cards worth knowing about. Whether you own them is your business.
TEN WORTH KNOWING

ANSWERS FIRST, THEN AMBITION

The first two close measured holes — the artifact zero and the smallest engine class. Seven more extend architecture the case files already signed, and the tenth closes nothing at all and says so on the page.

Zero. That is the count of artifact answers among all 80 entries, by enumeration of every oracle text in the deck, and it is the number that orders this page. Twelve removal copies sit above Walser's floor, so depth is not the problem — breadth is: two of five interaction classes covered, with Feed the Swarm reaching enchantments only.

The top two entries are the diagnosis's own standing adds. Gate to Phyrexia is two mana, repeats every upkeep, and is the only card on this list that moves the breadth count at all — 2 classes to 3 — which matters because a turn-two Grafdigger's Cage blanks the Gravecrawler recasts under both unbounded kills and every undying return under the Mikaeus and undying-pair lines. Culling the Weak is aimed at the smallest, lowest-rated engine class on the board: fast mana, three cards, drawn in 43.6% of games, the reason the commander lands on turn 2 in only 7.2% of sims. One body becomes BBBB for one mana. Its cost is stated in its entry, not hidden — ramp goes 12 to 13, one over the window. Both carry named natural cuts: Grave Pact and Victimize, one-for-one proposals, not a package.

Ranks three through nine extend architecture that is already verified. Bloodthirsty Conqueror doubles the scarce half of the deck's best commander-free kill — Exquisite Blood is one card drawn 6.6% unassisted, and the Conqueror is the same text on a 5/5 flying deathtoucher, opening three new two-card lines with drains already sleeved. Imp's Mischief is the one entry the protection count credits, 3 to 4, guarding the gate that three of the six kills pay: the commander on the engine turn. Sifter of Skulls and Altar of Dementia thicken the death-to-mana class at 61.2% and the free-outlet class at 78.5% respectively; Drivnod, Nether Traitor and Deathgreeter each buy a measured layer a little more slack.

The tenth, Walking Ballista, closes nothing any counted axis names, and its own entry says so — it is bought entirely on a two-card Mikaeus line nobody has put through the checker. Which is the honest note to end on: not one combo line these ten open is verified. Every one carries "needs a stack scenario", and that is a bill, not a footnote. The floor was recomputed rather than asserted — bracket 4 before, 4 after, on all ten — so nothing on this page changes the Rule 0 conversation.

The 99 is frozen as-played, so all ten are future unlocks ranked by what each would measurably buy. The top two are the diagnosis's own standing adds: the artifact class is zero by enumeration of all 80 entries and fast mana is the smallest, lowest-rated engine class at 43.6%. Ranks 3-9 extend verified architecture — three picks add two-card lines with drains already in the 99, two answer the commander gate that KILL D, E and F all pay, two thicken the death-to-mana class. The tenth closes nothing any measure names and says so out loud. Every bracket figure was recomputed with bracket.assess: floor 4 before and 4 after, on all ten.
  • 1. Gate to Phyrexia removal:spot
    ◆ EDHREC rank 15,886
    ★ A turn-two Grafdigger's Cage blanks the Gravecrawler recasts under KILL A and B and every undying return under D and F, and nothing among the 80 entries destroys or exiles an artifact at any price; Gate kills the Cage on your next upkeep for a body the nine-drain layer already pays you to sacrifice.
    When: The artifact class, repeatably, for two mana — the corpus says to 'cover the classes — creature, artifact, enchantment, graveyard, land — before doubling any', and artifact is the binding zero. Marginal frame: breadth goes 2 to 3 with this card and moves for no other card on this list. It is an enchantment, so Cursed Totem cannot switch off the thing that answers it. Priced honestly: your upkeep only, once each turn, so a resolved hoser gets one full rotation before it dies.
    Natural cut: Grave Pact
  • 2. Culling the Weak ramp:ritual
    ◆ EDHREC rank 551
    ★ Fast mana is the smallest and lowest-rated multi-card engine class — three cards, 43.6% drawn, the reason Yawgmoth lands on turn 2 in only 7.2% of sims — and Culling converts a body the deck draws in 82.3% of games into BBBB for one mana, a turn-2 commander or a same-turn Nest-plus-drain deploy.
    When: The turn the engine comes online moves up one: sacrifice the Stitcher's Supplier that already did its job and cast Yawgmoth with a Swamp open. The sacrifice is itself a death trigger for the drain layer. Stated cost: it carries ramp:ritual, so the ramp axis goes 12 to 13, one over the 10-12 window.
    Natural cut: Victimize
  • 3. Bloodthirsty Conqueror wincon:drain
    ◆ completes Bloodthirsty Conqueror + Marauding Blight-Priest (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ completes Bloodthirsty Conqueror + Starscape Cleric (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ completes Bloodthirsty Conqueror + South Wind Avatar (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ EDHREC rank 897
    ★ KILL C is the deck's best commander-free kill (mean turn 5.1, 42.6% assisted) and its 'opponent loses, you gain' half is one card, Exquisite Blood, at 6.6% unassisted; Bloodthirsty Conqueror is the same text on a 5/5 flying deathtoucher and pairs into a two-card loop with three drains already sleeved.
    When: Three new two-card lines with Marauding Blight-Priest, Starscape Cleric and South Wind Avatar — each the KILL C architecture with a second copy of the scarce half, so a graveyard lock that blanks the recursion kills leaves two amplifiers and three triggers instead of one and three. None is verified; each needs a stack scenario before it is claimed as a kill.
  • 4. Imp's Mischief protection:redirect
    ◆ EDHREC rank 685
    ★ Three of the six declared kills (D, E, F) need Yawgmoth on the battlefield, KILL E's 38.9% assisted by-turn-6 rate assumes he survives a rotation, and exactly three cards can carry him through a removal spell; Imp's Mischief bends a Swords to Plowshares onto another body at instant speed in a colour that cannot counter it.
    When: Marginal frame: it carries protection:redirect, so the axis the diagnosis calls a weakness goes 3 to 4 — the only card on this list the protection count credits. It also guards the other one-of the engine leans on: a targeted Nature's Claim on Nest of Scarabs, KILL E's irreplaceable piece, is a single-target spell and can be pushed elsewhere. Priced honestly: you lose life equal to the redirected spell's mana value, and it redirects only single-target spells.
  • 5. Walking Ballista removal:damage
    ◆ completes Walking Ballista + Mikaeus, the Unhallowed (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ EDHREC rank 441
    ★ Every current Mikaeus kill also needs the commander or a sac outlet on the board; Ballista plus Mikaeus alone is infinite pings at the table — remove the last counter, it dies at 0/0, undying returns it — so the deck gains a two-card kill that pays no commander tax when Yawgmoth has died twice.
    When: A removal piece and a mana sink stapled to a combo half: early it snipes a Viscera Seer's mirror or an Esper Sentinel, late every spare BB becomes counters. Read honestly: it carries no wincon role, so threat-density does not see it and no counted axis moves — this pick is bought entirely on the Mikaeus line, which needs a stack scenario before it is claimed.
  • 6. Drivnod, Carnage Dominus doubler:triggers
    ◆ completes Pitiless Plunderer + Reassembling Skeleton + Viscera Seer + Drivnod, Carnage Dominus (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ completes Pitiless Plunderer + Reassembling Skeleton + Carrion Feeder + Drivnod, Carnage Dominus (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ completes Pitiless Plunderer + Reassembling Skeleton + Blasting Station + Drivnod, Carnage Dominus (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ EDHREC rank 1,801
    ★ Every one of the nine drains, plus Pawn of Ulamog and Pitiless Plunderer, is a death-triggered ability, and Drivnod doubles all of them at once — a Gray Merchant board where each sacrifice drains twice, and a Butcher Ghoul + Geralf's Messenger loop (verified library-bounded and lethal in roughly thirty activations) that would need half the cycles, though that halving needs its own stack scenario.
    When: Four contained loops through Pitiless Plunderer and Reassembling Skeleton — the Blasting Station one produces infinite damage outright. His exile ability doubles as removal-proofing on himself. The doubling claim on any specific verified line stays unclaimed until a checker passes it.
  • 7. Altar of Dementia sac-outlet
    ◆ completes Mikaeus, the Unhallowed + Altar of Dementia (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ completes Gravecrawler + Pitiless Plunderer + Altar of Dementia (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ completes Pitiless Plunderer + Altar of Dementia + Reassembling Skeleton + Pawn of Ulamog (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ synergy: Woe Strider
    ◆ EDHREC rank 626
    ★ A ninth free repeatable outlet (the class sits at 78.5%) that is itself a two-card kill with Mikaeus — sacrifice him to his own undying, mill a library out — and a third piece the Gravecrawler + Pitiless Plunderer loop can route through when Warren Soultrader is answered.
    When: Three contained loops, including one that turns Pawn of Ulamog and Reassembling Skeleton into infinite mana and Treasure. Outside the combo it fills your own graveyard for Agadeem's Awakening and Phyrexian Reclamation, and it synergizes with Woe Strider's goat tokens as free fodder. Mill as a win reads honestly: one library at a time unless a loop is running.
  • 8. Sifter of Skulls ramp:dork
    ◆ completes Pitiless Plunderer + Viscera Seer + Reassembling Skeleton + Sifter of Skulls (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ completes Reassembling Skeleton + Phyrexian Altar + Sifter of Skulls (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ completes Yawgmoth, Thran Physician + Sifter of Skulls + Zulaport Cutthroat + Butcher Ghoul (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ EDHREC rank 3,486
    ★ The death-to-mana class is what makes a sacrifice loop mana-positive rather than merely free, and Sifter is a second Pawn of Ulamog — every nontoken death banks a sacrificial Scion that is both a colorless mana and one more body for Yawgmoth to spend.
    When: Seven contained lines, including a three-card loop with Reassembling Skeleton and Phyrexian Altar and a four-card near-infinite draw engine with the commander, Zulaport Cutthroat and Butcher Ghoul. Read honestly before buying: 'another nontoken creature' — Bitterblossom, Ophiomancer and Grave Titan tokens do not trigger it, so it is narrower here than its text looks in a creature deck.
  • 9. Nether Traitor recursion
    ◆ completes Nether Traitor + Phyrexian Altar + Pawn of Ulamog (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ completes Yahenni, Undying Partisan + Nether Traitor + Pawn of Ulamog + Pitiless Plunderer (needs a stack scenario)
    ◆ synergy: Stitcher's Supplier
    ◆ EDHREC rank 2,477
    ★ A tenth renewable-fodder source that recurs for B every time another creature dies — trivially every turn here — and both partners of its infinite-mana line, Phyrexian Altar and Pawn of Ulamog, are already sleeved.
    When: Two contained loops producing infinite colored mana and death triggers, plus a shadow body that connects for commander-damage chip when boards stall. Said plainly, because oracle text settles it: this is NOT Gravecrawler redundancy — Traitor triggers only when ANOTHER creature dies, so it cannot loop by sacrificing itself, and Gravecrawler's 17.0% single-point rate is untouched by this pick. Stitcher's Supplier milling it to the yard is a discount, not a cost.
  • 10. Deathgreeter threat:body
    ◆ EDHREC rank 5,216
    ★ In this deck life is the real spell resource — Yawgmoth's draw, Necropotence, Ancient Tomb and two Phyrexian pips all charge it — and a one-drop that banks a life for every creature death, yours or the table's, refuels roughly four activations a turn once the engine runs.
    When: Longer Yawgmoth chains on grind turns and a safer Necropotence number at end of turn. Ranked last on purpose, and honestly: it carries only threat:body, no counted axis moves for it, and it closes nothing the diagnosis names — it holds a slot on this list because roughly half the commander's community lists run it, and the strongest thing this page can do with a famous card the measures cannot see is say exactly that.
1
THE AXIS THAT ACTUALLY BINDSInteraction-breadth reads 2 of 5 classes, and the artifact class is zero by enumeration of all 80 entries' oracle text — in a format whose cheap hosers for exactly this deck are artifacts. Gate to Phyrexia is the one card on the list that moves the count, 2 to 3, and it is an enchantment, so the Totem it answers cannot switch it off.
2
THE SMALLEST CLASS ON THE SHEETFast mana is three cards at 43.6% — the reason the commander lands on turn two in only 7.2% of sims. Culling the Weak converts a body the deck draws in 82.3% of games into BBBB for one mana. Its cost is stated, not hidden: the ramp count goes one over its window.
3
NOT ONE LINE HERE IS SIGNEDEvery combo a pick would open carries 'needs a stack scenario' by construction. Two are worth a resolve run before any purchase: Bloodthirsty Conqueror beside the three lifegain drains already sleeved, because the whole case is that it doubles Exquisite Blood's scarce half; and Walking Ballista with Mikaeus, because that pick is bought entirely on a line nobody has put through the checker.
4
HONEST LAST PLACEDeathgreeter closes nothing any counted axis names, and its entry says so out loud — it holds a slot because roughly half the commander's community lists run it, and the strongest thing a ranked page can do with a famous card the measures cannot see is say exactly that. The floor was recomputed on all ten: bracket 4 before, 4 after, every time.

Judge's Desk

by Counselor Vera Dictum
✓ RULES-VERIFIED
The full case files. The Counselor read them twice.
THE CASE FILES

ELEVEN FILES, EVERY QUOTE VERBATIM

Eleven complete resolutions, the checker's verdict as case status, every citation reproduced exactly as the Comprehensive Rules prints it. You do not have to believe this magazine. You can read the record.

Every claim the magazine made, with the rule text that backs it. Nothing here is paraphrased. Tap a case to open its full record.

Case A-002 Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs + Bastion of Remembrance: what bounds the engine?
Rules version June 19, 2026 · status: cleared in 3 review cycle(s) · tap to open Verified
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then run the engine as far as the rules allow. (a) Is this loop unbounded? Name precisely what Yawgmoth's ability can legally target on each iteration, given that Nest of Scarabs' replacement Insect does not exist when the target is chosen, and state the exact number of completed activations from this board and what ends it. (b) What is the net change to your life total per completed activation, and how much total life do all four opponents lose?
  1. Fix the reading of the board before resolving anything, then account for what already happened during the activation itself. THE READING: the scenario's board line for Insect token A is annotated 'already sacrificed to pay the cost of the ability now on the stack', which matches stack[0] ('1 life paid (you are at 39) and Insect token A sacrificed') and the stated life total of 39. Insect A is therefore in the graveyard, not on the battlefield, and the creatures available to pay future sacrifice costs are Insect token B and the Human Soldier token - a THREE-body board counting the one already spent (A, B, Soldier), not four. Every figure below follows from that reading. Within the activation itself the order was: Yawgmoth's ability was put on the stack, its target (Grizzly Bears) was chosen, and only then were its costs paid - 1 life (you 40 -> 39) and the sacrifice of Insect token A.
    Insect token A was a creature you controlled that died, so Bastion of Remembrance's ability ('Whenever a creature you control dies, each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life') has triggered. This is a leaves-the-battlefield trigger and it looks back in time: the game consults the game state immediately before the sacrifice to see that the dying Insect was a creature YOU controlled, which is what licenses this trigger and the six that follow it - SEVEN Bastion triggers in all across the five completed activations. It triggered during the process of activating an ability, so it did nothing at that moment; it waits and is put on the stack the next time a player would receive priority, which is immediately after the activation is complete. It goes on top of Yawgmoth's ability. That the sacrificed creature was a TOKEN does not suppress this: a token that changes zones triggers applicable triggered abilities before it ceases to exist - the point that carries every one of the seven drains in this line, since all seven creatures of yours that die here are tokens. Stack, bottom to top: [0] Yawgmoth's ability targeting Grizzly Bears, [1] Bastion's drain trigger.
    CR 602.2b — “The remainder of the process for activating an ability is identical to the process for casting a spell listed in rules 601.2b–i.”
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard. A player can’t sacrifice something that isn’t a permanent, or something that’s a permanent they don’t control.”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers. The ability doesn’t do anything at this point.”
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority. See rule 117, “Timing and Priority.” The ability becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
    CR 603.10a — “Some zone-change triggers look back in time. These are leaves-the-battlefield abilities, abilities that trigger when a player sacrifices a permanent”
    CR 111.7 — “Note that if a token changes zones, applicable triggered abilities will trigger before the token ceases to exist.”
  2. All players pass; the top object - Bastion of Remembrance's drain trigger - resolves. This is drain #1.
    Each of the four opponents loses 1 life: 40 -> 39 each. You gain 1 life: 39 -> 40. Opposing life in the pod: 160 -> 156.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  3. All players pass again; Yawgmoth's activated ability resolves. 'Put a -1/-1 counter on up to one target creature and draw a card.' Target: Grizzly Bears.
    You, as the controller of the ability, follow its instructions in the order written: YOU put one -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears, which becomes 1/1, and YOU draw a card (library 60 -> 59, hand 0 -> 1). The identity of the player placing the counter is the whole trigger condition of Nest of Scarabs, which reads 'Whenever YOU put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature, create that many 1/1 black Insect creature tokens' - and because the ability's controller is you, that condition is met and Nest triggers for one counter. It has triggered and has done nothing yet - no token exists.
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 122.1a — “A +X/+Y counter on a creature or on a creature card in a zone other than the battlefield, where X and Y are numbers, adds X to that object’s power and Y to that object’s toughness. Similarly, -X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
    CR 121.1 — “A player draws a card by putting the top card of their library into their hand.”
  4. A player would receive priority, so the game runs state-based actions first, repeats until none is performed, and only then puts waiting triggered abilities on the stack.
    The state-based action check finds nothing to do: Grizzly Bears' toughness is 1, and the state-based action that moves a creature to its owner's graveyard applies only at toughness 0 or less (quoted at step 5, where it is met). Nest of Scarabs' trigger is then put on the stack and resolves, creating Insect token C under your control. ACTIVATION 1 COMPLETE. Bodies you control other than Yawgmoth: Insect B, Human Soldier token, Insect C = 3 (you spent Insect A and got Insect C: net zero, because the counter went onto an opponent's creature). You: 40 life, 59 cards in library, 1 card in hand. Opponents: 39 each.
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack (see rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities”).”
  5. ACTIVATION 2, same shape: activate Yawgmoth targeting Grizzly Bears again, pay 1 life (40 -> 39), sacrifice Insect B. Bastion's trigger from Insect B's death resolves first (drain #2: opponents 39 -> 38, you 39 -> 40), then Yawgmoth's ability resolves, putting a second -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears (now 0/0) and drawing a card.
    Nest of Scarabs triggers again but has not resolved. Before that trigger can be put on the stack, state-based actions run: Grizzly Bears has toughness 0 and is put into its owner's graveyard - it dies. Bastion of Remembrance does NOT trigger for it: Bastion reads 'Whenever a creature you control dies', and the look-back the trigger performs (step 1) finds Grizzly Bears under opponent A's control at the moment it left the battlefield. Only then is Nest's trigger put on the stack; it resolves and creates Insect token D. This is the same ordering that refuted the near-infinite claim in stack 001: the Insect that REPLACES THE BODY YOU SPENT (Nest is a triggered ability, not a replacement effect - nothing here is replaced in the 614 sense) is created strictly after the death it makes up for, and it never exists while that iteration's target is being chosen. ACTIVATION 2 COMPLETE. Bodies other than Yawgmoth: Human Soldier, Insect C, Insect D = 3 (again net zero - the opponent's creature absorbed the counter). You: 40 life, library 58, 2 cards in hand. Opponents: 38 each.
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack (see rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities”).”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 700.4 — “The term dies means “is put into a graveyard from the battlefield.””
  6. Census of legal targets for every subsequent activation, now that no opponent controls a creature. Targets for an activated ability are chosen as the ability is activated, from what exists at that moment.
    Three candidates are ruled out and one class remains. (i) Yawgmoth himself is NEVER a legal target of his own ability: he has protection from Humans and he is a Human Cleric, so the ability's source has the stated quality and cannot target him. (ii) Bastion of Remembrance and Nest of Scarabs are Enchantments, not creatures, so they are not 'target creature' at all. (iii) The Insect that Nest of Scarabs will create does not exist when the target is chosen, so it can never be the target of the same activation that creates it. What remains: only the 1/1 tokens you already control - the Human Soldier token (a Human, but protection is a property of Yawgmoth, not a restriction on whom Yawgmoth may target) and any Insect tokens from earlier iterations. Every one of them dies to a single counter, which is what turns the engine body-negative at step 7.
    CR 702.16b — “A permanent or player with protection can’t be targeted by spells with the stated quality and can’t be targeted by abilities from a source with the stated quality.”
    CR 115.1c — “An activated ability is targeted if it identifies something it will affect by using the phrase “target [something],” where the “something” is a phrase that describes an object and/or player. The target(s) are chosen as the ability is activated; see rule 602.2b.”
  7. ACTIVATIONS 3 and 4 - the true engine iteration, where the counter must land on one of your own bodies. Activation 3: pay 1 life (40 -> 39), sacrifice Insect C, target Insect D. Activation 4: pay 1 life (41 -> 40), sacrifice the Human Soldier token, target Insect E.
    Each such activation produces TWO Bastion drains, not one. First the sacrificed creature dies during cost payment (drain), and that trigger resolves before Yawgmoth's ability. Then Yawgmoth's ability resolves and puts a -1/-1 counter on your own 1/1, making it 0/0; Nest of Scarabs triggers. State-based actions then kill that 1/1 - a second creature you control dying, so Bastion triggers again (a token still triggers this before it ceases to exist). Bastion's new trigger and Nest's waiting trigger go on the stack together and you order them freely; both resolve. Net per iteration: minus 2 bodies (one sacrificed, one counter-killed) plus 1 Insect = NET MINUS ONE BODY; 2 drains minus the 1 life paid as a cost (the paying-life rule is cited at step 11, where the totals land) = NET PLUS 1 LIFE for you and MINUS 2 for each opponent. Activation 3 (drains #3, #4): opponents 38 -> 36, you 39 -> 41; bodies left = Human Soldier, Insect E = 2. Activation 4 (drains #5, #6): opponents 36 -> 34, you 40 -> 42; bodies left = Insect F = 1. Library 56, 4 cards in hand.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 111.7 — “A token that’s in a zone other than the battlefield ceases to exist. This is a state-based action; see rule 704. (Note that if a token changes zones, applicable triggered abilities will trigger before the token ceases to exist.)”
    CR 603.3b — “First, each player, in APNAP order, puts each triggered ability they control with a trigger condition that isn’t another ability triggering on the stack in any order they choose.”
  8. ACTIVATION 5 - one body left (Insect F), and it is the same body the cost must eat. Targets are announced during the proposal of the ability and costs are paid afterward, so Insect F is still on the battlefield and IS a legal target choice when targets are declared. Two lines exist; take the better one.
    Line A (target Insect F, then sacrifice it to pay the cost): on resolution the ability's only target is no longer in the zone it was in, every instance of 'target' is illegal, and the ability does not resolve - no counter, no Nest trigger, and NO CARD DRAWN. Line B (declare zero targets, which 'up to one target creature' expressly permits - an ability that allows zero targets to be chosen is not targeted at all when none is chosen, so the target-legality check on resolution never bites): the ability resolves, the counter instruction does nothing for want of a target, and you still draw a card; Nest of Scarabs does not trigger because no counter was put on anything, so no Insect replaces Insect F. Line B is strictly better and is the line taken. Either way Insect F's sacrifice gives exactly ONE Bastion drain (#7): pay 1 life (42 -> 41), drain back to 42; opponents 34 -> 33. ACTIVATION 5 COMPLETE. Library 55, 5 cards in hand. Bodies other than Yawgmoth: ZERO.
    CR 601.2c — “If the spell has a variable number of targets, the player announces how many targets they will choose before they announce those targets.”
    CR 601.2h — “The player pays the total cost. First, they pay all costs that don’t involve random elements or moving objects from the library to a public zone, in any order.”
    CR 115.6 — “A spell or ability that requires targets may allow zero targets to be chosen. Such a spell or ability is still said to require targets, but that spell or ability is targeted only if one or more targets have been chosen for it.”
    CR 608.2b — “If the spell or ability specifies targets, it checks whether the targets are still legal. A target that’s no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted is illegal. Other changes to the game state may cause a target to no longer be legal; for example, its characteristics may have changed or an effect may have changed the text of the spell. If the source of an ability has left the zone it was in, its last known information is used during this process. If all its targets, for every instance of the word “target,” are now illegal, the spell or ability doesn’t resolve.”
  9. Attempt a sixth activation of the SACRIFICE ability. The only creature on the battlefield is Yawgmoth himself, and his cost reads 'Sacrifice another creature'.
    THE SACRIFICE LOOP IS BOUNDED AND IT ENDS HERE, on running out of creatures to sacrifice. You control no creature other than Yawgmoth, so the sacrifice cost is unpayable; partial payment is not allowed, and an activation begun and not legally completable is reversed in full with no abilities triggering. This exhausts the FIRST ability only - Yawgmoth's second ability, '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate', is still activatable here (three untapped Swamps, five cards in hand); in this line it would simply accomplish nothing, because no permanent and no player carries a counter once the Bears and every counter-bearing token are gone. Note precisely what did NOT end it: (i) not your life - you paid 5 life across five activations and gained 7, ending HIGHER than you started; (ii) not your library - 55 cards remain and the draw-from-an-empty-library loss never comes near; (iii) not the opponent's creature toughness - Grizzly Bears' 2 toughness only EXTENDED the engine, absorbing two counters and letting activations 1 and 2 cost zero net bodies; (iv) not running out of targets in the abstract, since your own Insects are always legal targets - you run out of bodies. The general rule for this board: completed activations = (sacrificeable bodies you start with) + (total toughness of opposing creatures you can put counters on) = 3 + 2 = 5.
    CR 601.2h — “Partial payments are not allowed. Unpayable costs can’t be paid.”
    CR 733.1 — “If a player takes an illegal action or starts to take an action but can’t legally complete it, the entire action is reversed and any payments already made are canceled.”
    CR 121.4 — “A player who attempts to draw a card from a library with no cards in it loses the game the next time a player would receive priority.”
  10. SEPARATE THE BOARD INVARIANT FROM THE LINE. Everything above is the sacrifice engine on its own; the board also offers Yawgmoth's SECOND ability, '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate', which the three Swamps can pay exactly once (they make {B}{B}{B}, and there is no second {B}{B} after that) and which has a card to discard from activation 1 onward. It does not change the number of completed sacrifice activations, and it does change the drain count.
    BOARD INVARIANT (sacrifice engine alone, no proliferate): 5 completed activations, 7 Bastion triggers, 28 life removed from the pod - the figure reported in final_state. Sister stack 003 also lands on 28, but NOT on this board and NOT by this route, and the difference is the single most useful thing these two stacks teach together. 003 swaps BASTION OF REMEMBRANCE, an enchantment, for ZULAPORT CUTTHROAT, a 1/1 CREATURE, and has no Human Soldier token. That is forced: an enchantment cannot be a body, so this board's third sacrificeable body is the 1/1 white Human Soldier that Bastion's own enters-the-battlefield ability made, while on 003 the drainer IS the third body. Two consequences follow. (i) 003's drainer can die, and the Insect that Nest returns in its place cannot drain, so 003 caps at SEVEN drains even WITH a proliferate; this board's drainer is an enchantment that can never die, which is exactly why one proliferate here reaches EIGHT. (ii) The two 28s are not the same quantity reached the same way: 003's 28 is its ceiling INCLUDING one proliferate, while the 28 here is the NO-proliferate figure and this board still has 32 above it. The figures coincide only because both boards happen to offer three sacrificeable bodies and the same 2-toughness Grizzly Bears, so both run five activations - coincidence of arithmetic, not corroboration, and neither number checks the other. LINE-DEPENDENT VARIANT (one proliferate, taken after activation 1): proliferating the Grizzly Bears - the only permanent then carrying a counter - gives it a second -1/-1 counter. You, as the controller of that ability, are the one putting the counter, so Nest of Scarabs triggers for one Insect exactly as it does off Yawgmoth's first ability, and the Bears dies to a state-based action without any of your creatures being spent on it. That converts the Bears' remaining toughness into a BODY instead of into a free activation: the sacrifice-activation count is still FIVE, but one more iteration now lands its counter on your own creature, giving 8 Bastion drains instead of 7 (you 43, each opponent 32, 32 total drained, library 55, hand 4 after the discard). The two figures must not be blended: 7 / 28 is a property of the board, 8 / 32 is a property of a line that spends {B}{B} and a card.
    CR 701.34a — “To proliferate means to choose any number of permanents and/or players that have a counter, then give each one additional counter of each kind that permanent or player already has.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
  11. Final accounting over all five completed activations of the sacrifice ability (board-invariant line, no proliferate).
    SEVEN creatures you controlled died: Insect A, Insect B, Insect C, the Human Soldier token and Insect F sacrificed as costs (5), plus Insect D and Insect E counter-killed by state-based action on activations 3 and 4 (2). Bastion of Remembrance therefore triggered SEVEN times, one per death. All seven were TOKENS, and the sequence is the mechanism that pays this deck, so state it in order rather than in reverse: each of the seven DID reach the graveyard - the five sacrifices are moved from the battlefield directly to their owner's graveyard by 701.21a (cited at step 1) and the two counter-killed Insects are put there by 704.5f (cited at steps 5 and 7) - and that arrival IS the 'dies' event of 700.4 (cited at step 5), which is the trigger condition every one of the seven Bastion drains keys on. Only afterwards, on the next state-based-action check (117.5, cited at steps 4 and 5), does 704.5d apply: each token is by then in a zone other than the battlefield and ceases to exist. It dies first and ceases to exist second. So the ending graveyard holds none of them and gains no card - graveyard_additions is empty as an END STATE, not because nothing of yours ever reached the graveyard. LIFE: you paid 1 life five times and gained 1 life seven times; the gain-and-lose adjustment for a Bastion drain is established at step 2 and every one of the seven drains has that identical shape. From the 40 you had before activation 1 (39 as the scenario states it, with the first payment already made) you finish at 42 - net +2 over the whole engine, +3 from the stated board. Per completed activation the net change to YOUR life is: 0 on activations 1, 2 and 5 (one drain, one life paid) and +1 on activations 3 and 4, which are the only iterations running in true engine mode (two drains, one life paid). Each of the four opponents loses exactly 7 life, 40 -> 33. Total life removed from the pod: 7 x 4 = 28, from 160 down to 132. LETHALITY, stated as a checked state-based action rather than an impression: the life-total check runs each time a player would receive priority (117.5, step 4) and never fires anywhere in this line - it takes a player at 0 or less life, and the lowest total on the table at any point is 33. Nobody is close to dead; this is a value engine and a sacrifice outlet, not a kill.
    CR 118.3b — “Paying life is done by subtracting the indicated amount of life from a player’s life total.”
    CR 704.5d — “If a token is in a zone other than the battlefield, it ceases to exist.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
After the line
Your life
42
Your battlefield
Yawgmoth, Thran Physician (2/4, untapped, no counters; his SACRIFICE ability is no longer activatable for want of another creature, but '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate' remains activatable and would simply do nothing here, since no permanent or player carries a counter), Nest of Scarabs, Bastion of Remembrance, Swamp (untapped), Swamp (untapped), Swamp (untapped)
Opponents
Opponent A: 33, Opponent B: 33, Opponent C: 33, Opponent D: 33

Case A-003 Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs + Zulaport Cutthroat: does a creature drain survive its own engine?
Rules version June 19, 2026 · status: cleared in 3 review cycle(s) · tap to open Verified
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then run the engine as far as the rules allow, playing to kill the table. (a) Zulaport Cutthroat is a CREATURE and therefore a legal target for Yawgmoth's ability and a legal sacrifice for its cost. State whether the pilot is ever FORCED to consume it, or whether it can be preserved throughout, and give the exact number of completed activations from this board. (b) Zulaport drains EACH opponent. How much total life do all four opponents lose, and is that lethal?
  1. Fix the reading first, then do the bookkeeping the stack picture omits. THE READING: the scenario's board annotates 'Insect token A' as already sacrificed to pay the cost of the ability now on the stack, matching the house convention used in stack 001 — the board list names the cost-payment body, it is NOT still on the battlefield, and the pilot's live insect count entering this resolution is ONE (Insect token B). Every number below is derived under that reading; the n=4 reading in which both A and B are alive is not the scenario as written. BOOKKEEPING: paying the cost sacrificed Insect token A, moving it from the battlefield to the graveyard, which is a 'dies' event. Zulaport Cutthroat's ability is a look-back-in-time trigger and sees that death even though Insect A is a TOKEN and a later state-based action removes it from the graveyard. That trigger is put on the stack ABOVE the activated ability and therefore resolves first.
    Stack (top-down): Zulaport Cutthroat's death trigger; Yawgmoth's -1/-1 ability targeting Grizzly Bears. You are at 39 (1 life paid); opponents at 40 each. Insect A reached your graveyard (making it a legal 'dies' event) and then ceased to exist as a token — which is why it will never appear in the final graveyard list even though it drained the table.
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 700.4 — “The term dies means “is put into a graveyard from the battlefield.””
    CR 704.5d — “If a token is in a zone other than the battlefield, it ceases to exist.”
    CR 603.10a — “Some zone-change triggers look back in time. These are leaves-the-battlefield abilities, abilities that trigger when a player sacrifices a permanent”
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority.”
  2. Zulaport Cutthroat's trigger resolves: 'each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life.' Four opponents, so four separate life losses.
    Opponent A/B/C/D: 40 -> 39 each (4 total life lost). You: 39 -> 40. Death count of creatures you control: 1.
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  3. Yawgmoth's activated ability resolves. Before it does anything, its target is rechecked for legality: Grizzly Bears is still on the battlefield and nothing has changed its characteristics, so it is still a legal target and the ability resolves normally. Put one -1/-1 counter on it and draw a card.
    Grizzly Bears is 1/1 with one -1/-1 counter — toughness above 0, so it survives. Library 60 -> 59, hand 1. Putting that counter triggers Nest of Scarabs.
    CR 608.2b — “If the spell or ability specifies targets, it checks whether the targets are still legal.”
    CR 122.1a — “Similarly, -X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
  4. State-based actions are checked (nothing dies: Bears is 1/1), then Nest of Scarabs' trigger is put on the stack. All players pass priority in succession — the scenario's stated assumption — so it resolves, creating one 1/1 black Insect token (Insect C). This same all-pass condition is what lets every object that resolves across this main phase actually resolve, and it is not restated below.
    Your board: Yawgmoth 2/4, Nest of Scarabs, Zulaport Cutthroat 1/1, Insect B, Insect C. Critically, Insect C could NOT have been the target of the activation that produced it — targets for that ability were announced when it was put on the stack, when Insect C did not exist. This is the same lock that refuted stack 001.
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack (see rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities”).”
    CR 117.4 — “If all players pass in succession (that is, if all players pass without taking any actions in between passing), the spell or ability on top of the stack resolves”
    CR 601.2c — “The player announces their choice of an appropriate object or player for each target the spell requires.”
    CR 602.2b — “The remainder of the process for activating an ability is identical to the process for casting a spell listed in rules 601.2b–i. Those rules apply to activating an ability just as they apply to casting a spell.”
  5. Answer (a), the load-bearing question: is the pilot ever FORCED to consume Zulaport Cutthroat? No — on three independent grounds. (i) Yawgmoth's ability is an ACTIVATED ability: the pilot may activate it, and is never compelled to, unlike stack 001's mandatory triggered ability. (ii) The cost is 'Sacrifice another creature': the pilot pays the cost and any creature they control other than Yawgmoth is a full payment, so an Insect token satisfies it whenever one exists. (iii) The effect is 'up to one target creature', so choosing ZERO targets is legal — the counter can never be forced onto Zulaport.
    Zulaport Cutthroat is preserved through every iteration in which any Insect token exists. It is consumed only at the very end, and only because the pilot ELECTS to cash it in for one final drain once it is the last sacrificeable body. Nothing in the engine can take it involuntarily.
    CR 113.3b — “A player may activate such an ability whenever they have priority.”
    CR 601.2h — “The player pays the total cost.”
    CR 115.6 — “A spell or ability that requires targets may allow zero targets to be chosen.”
  6. Establish the loop's hard ceiling before running it. The engine is body-neutral only while some creature can absorb a -1/-1 counter and live: each activation spends one body (sacrifice) and Nest returns at most one body (one counter, one token). Every creature you control except Yawgmoth is a 1/1, so a counter on your own board kills the body it lands on. Yawgmoth is 2/4 and could absorb three counters — but he has protection from Humans and the source of his own activated ability is Yawgmoth, a Human Cleric, so he can never be targeted by it.
    The only sink that can take a counter and survive is Grizzly Bears (2/2), and it can absorb exactly two -1/-1 counters before dying. After Bears is gone every activation is net -1 body. The loop is therefore strictly FINITE, not 'near-infinite'.
    CR 702.16b — “A permanent or player with protection can’t be targeted by spells with the stated quality and can’t be targeted by abilities from a source with the stated quality.”
    CR 113.7 — “The source of an activated ability on the stack is the object whose ability was activated.”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  7. Convert the three untapped Swamps — the resource the scenario deliberately supplies, since the -1/-1 ability costs no mana. Activate Yawgmoth's second ability: {B}{B}, Discard a card (the card drawn in step 3): Proliferate. Choose Grizzly Bears, the only permanent in the game with a counter on it, and give it a second -1/-1 counter.
    Bears becomes 0/0. You put a -1/-1 counter on a creature, so Nest of Scarabs triggers. By the step 4 sequencing, SBAs run first and Bears is put into its owner's graveyard (704.5f, established in step 6) — it is an OPPONENT'S creature and Zulaport reads 'another creature YOU control', so Zulaport does not trigger, and Bears is a card, not a token, so it stays in the graveyard — then the Nest trigger resolves for Insect token D. Net: one extra body for {B}{B} and a discard, with no sacrifice spent. Mana remaining {B} (a second proliferate is unaffordable). Your bodies: Insect B, C, D + Zulaport = 4.
    CR 701.34a — “To proliferate means to choose any number of permanents and/or players that have a counter, then give each one additional counter of each kind that permanent or player already has.”
  8. Activation #2 of the -1/-1 ability, and the step that puts the TOKEN-DEATH mechanic on the record. Pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect B (a token — it moves to the graveyard, which is the 'dies' event, and only ceases to exist afterwards), target Insect C. On resolution, put a -1/-1 counter on Insect C and draw. Insect C is now 0/0, and SBAs run as a single event: 704.5f puts Insect C into its owner's graveyard, and only on the NEXT SBA check — the repeat that 117.5 requires — is 704.5d applicable, because the token is only then in a zone other than the battlefield. That ordering is why each token death is a real 'dies' event Zulaport can see; a token that never reached the graveyard would produce one drain in this whole line, not seven. Two abilities have now triggered since the last priority — Zulaport's second death trigger and Nest of Scarabs' token trigger — and the pilot controls both, so the pilot chooses the order they go on the stack; both orders reach the same totals here, because Nest's replacement token can never become the target of an activation whose targets were already locked (step 4). This same simultaneous-trigger choice recurs in step 9.
    Two drains this activation. Opponents 39 -> 37 each; you 40 -> 41 (paid 1, gained 2). Library 58, hand 1. Insect B and Insect C both hit the graveyard and both ceased to exist. Nest's trigger creates Insect E. Bodies: Insect D, Insect E + Zulaport. Death count 3.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 704.5d — “If a token is in a zone other than the battlefield, it ceases to exist.”
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack (see rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities”).”
    CR 603.3b — “First, each player, in APNAP order, puts each triggered ability they control with a trigger condition that isn’t another ability triggering on the stack in any order they choose.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  9. Activation #3, identical shape to step 8 in every respect — same sacrifice-death, same 704.5f-then-704.5d token disposal, same pilot-ordered simultaneous triggers, same life adjustments. Pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect D, target Insect E; the counter takes Insect E to 0/0 and it is put into the graveyard; Nest creates Insect F.
    Two more drains. Opponents 37 -> 35 each; you 41 -> 42. Library 57, hand 2. Bodies: Insect F + Zulaport. Death count 5.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  10. Activation #4. Pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect F. Now the target choice matters: the only creatures left would be Zulaport Cutthroat and Yawgmoth. Yawgmoth cannot be targeted, and the pilot declines to kill Zulaport, so choose ZERO targets — legal because the ability reads 'up to one target creature'. With no target chosen there is nothing for the on-resolution legality check of step 3 to examine, so the ability resolves and simply draws.
    One drain (Insect F, a token death by sacrifice as in step 1). No counter is placed, so Nest of Scarabs does not trigger and no token is made. Opponents 35 -> 34 each; you 42 -> 42 (paid 1, gained 1). Library 56, hand 3. Bodies: Zulaport Cutthroat only. Death count 6. (Targeting Zulaport here instead would trade the same total — it dies for a drain and Nest replaces it with a body that can no longer drain — so 7 is the ceiling either way.)
    CR 115.6 — “A spell or ability that requires targets may allow zero targets to be chosen.”
  11. Activation #5, the final one, and the literal subject of the scenario's title: does the drain survive its own engine? Pay 1 life, sacrifice Zulaport Cutthroat itself. Its ability reads 'Whenever this creature or another creature you control dies' — a leaves-the-battlefield ability, and such abilities look back in time to the game state immediately before the event, so Zulaport sees the very sacrifice that removed it and its trigger goes on the stack. Zero targets again (only Yawgmoth remains and he is untargetable). Draw. Then the loop ENDS: Yawgmoth is the only creature you control and the cost demands ANOTHER creature, so activation #6 has no legal payment.
    Seventh and last drain. Opponents 34 -> 33 each; you 42. Library 55, hand 4. Zulaport is a card, not a token, so it remains in the graveyard. Board: Yawgmoth, Nest of Scarabs, three Swamps. Total completed activations: FIVE of the -1/-1 ability (including the one that started on the stack) plus ONE proliferate activation. What ends the loop is body exhaustion under 'Sacrifice ANOTHER creature' — not life, not the library.
    CR 603.6c — “Leaves-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent moves from the battlefield to another zone, or when a phased-in permanent leaves the game because its owner leaves the game.”
    CR 603.10a — “Some zone-change triggers look back in time. These are leaves-the-battlefield abilities, abilities that trigger when a player sacrifices a permanent”
    CR 118.3 — “A player can’t pay a cost without having the necessary resources to pay it fully.”
    CR 601.2h — “Unpayable costs can’t be paid.”
  12. Final state-based-action check and the arithmetic for (b). Seven creatures you controlled died while Zulaport Cutthroat was on the battlefield or, on the last one, was looking back at its own departure (Insect A, B, C, D, E, F and Zulaport itself). Each death drained EACH of four opponents for 1. Grizzly Bears is excluded: it belongs to opponent A and Zulaport reads 'another creature YOU control'.
    Each opponent loses 7 life: 40 -> 33. Total life removed from the table: 7 x 4 = 28. Opposing life 160 -> 132. No opponent is at 0 or less life, so no player loses the game; you never came near decking (60 -> 55). NOT lethal, and not close — the line removes 17.5% of the table's life. The 'near-infinite' family tag is wrong here for the second time on this deck.
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
After the line
Your life
42
Your battlefield
Yawgmoth, Thran Physician (2/4, no counters), Nest of Scarabs, Swamp, Swamp, Swamp
Opponents
Opponent A: 33, Opponent B: 33, Opponent C: 33, Opponent D: 33

Case A-004 Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs + Blood Artist: can a single-target drain close a four-player table?
Rules version June 19, 2026 · status: cleared in 4 review cycle(s) · tap to open Verified
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then run the engine as far as the rules allow. (a) Blood Artist's trigger reads 'target player loses 1 life and you gain 1 life' — a single target chosen per trigger. State the exact number of completed activations from this board and what ends it. (b) Given that each trigger drains only ONE player, how much life can be removed in total, and can this line reduce all four opponents from 40 to 0?
  1. Nothing has resolved yet; put the waiting triggered ability on the stack — but state-based actions go FIRST. Insect token A was sacrificed to pay Yawgmoth's activation cost, which moved it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard, i.e. it died, and Blood Artist triggered on that death. No player had priority during the activation, so the trigger has been waiting. Each time a player would get priority the game performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event and repeats until none are performed, and ONLY THEN are waiting triggered abilities put on the stack.
    Order on this board: (i) Insect A is in the graveyard; (ii) SBA check — a token in a zone other than the battlefield ceases to exist, so Insect A CEASES TO EXIST here, before any trigger reaches the stack; (iii) the check is repeated, nothing further is performed, and Blood Artist's waiting trigger is then put on the stack above Yawgmoth's ability, its one target player chosen at that moment (targets are declared as part of putting an ability on the stack — cited at step 11, where that timing does the work). Insect A ceasing to exist does not undo the trigger — the ability had already triggered on the death. Stack, bottom to top: (pos 0) Yawgmoth's activated ability targeting Grizzly Bears; (pos 1) Blood Artist's trigger for Insect A's death, targeting opponent A. All players pass priority.
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 700.4 — “The term dies means “is put into a graveyard from the battlefield.””
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack”
    CR 704.5d — “If a token is in a zone other than the battlefield, it ceases to exist.”
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority.”
  2. Resolve the top object: Blood Artist's trigger for Insect A's death.
    DRAIN 1. Opponent A loses 1 life (40 -> 39). You gain 1 life (39 -> 40). Note the shape that decides this whole scenario: ONE player lost 1 life, not each opponent. Opponents B, C and D are untouched.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
  3. Resolve pos 0: Yawgmoth's activated ability, putting a -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears and drawing a card.
    Grizzly Bears becomes 1/1 and SURVIVES — this is the only creature on the table that can absorb a counter and live, and that fact is the entire engine. You draw a card (library 60 -> 59, hand 0 -> 1). Because YOU put a -1/-1 counter on a creature, Nest of Scarabs triggers — and the actor is established, not assumed: this is your activated ability and the controller of an ability is the player who follows its instructions, so the player putting the counter on is you. Nest of Scarabs' trigger condition is literally 'Whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature', so the identity of that actor is the whole of the condition. State-based actions are checked first: Grizzly Bears at toughness 1 is not put into a graveyard, nothing else is illegal. Nest's trigger is then put on the stack and resolves: create Insect token C. Your Insects: B and C.
    CR 122.1a — “Similarly, -X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 704.3 — “If any state-based actions are performed as a result of a check, the check is repeated; otherwise all triggered abilities that are waiting to be put on the stack are put on the stack, then the check is repeated.”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  4. STRUCTURAL FINDING — why the engine is body-negative once Grizzly Bears is gone. The cause is ARITHMETIC, not timing: the activation cost is one of your creatures and Nest of Scarabs returns exactly one Insect per counter placed, so the moment the counter also kills one of YOUR creatures, two of your creatures leave and one token arrives — net minus one. That holds identically even if the token appeared instantly; the token's lateness is a true but separate fact and is NOT the cause of the body loss.
    What the timing does cost you is a target, not a body. Targets for an activated ability are chosen as the ability is ACTIVATED and the cost is paid later in the same process, so at the instant you choose a target (i) the creature you are about to sacrifice is still on the battlefield but will be gone at resolution, and (ii) the Insect that Nest is about to make does not exist yet and therefore can never be that same activation's target. Taxonomy for this board, stated exactly: an activation targeting one of YOUR 1/1 Insects (or the 0/1 Blood Artist) is minus one body and yields 2 drains; an activation targeting Grizzly Bears while it already holds one counter is BODY-NEUTRAL for you — you sacrifice one, Nest returns one, and the Bears is not your creature — and yields 2 drains; an activation targeting Grizzly Bears at 2/2 (it survives) is body-neutral and yields 1 drain; an activation choosing zero targets is minus one body, makes no token, and yields 1 drain. Grizzly Bears' toughness of 2 supplies exactly ONE survivable counter, and step 3 has already spent it.
    CR 115.1c — “The target(s) are chosen as the ability is activated; see rule 602.2b.”
    CR 602.2b — “The remainder of the process for activating an ability is identical to the process for casting a spell listed in rules 601.2b–i.”
    CR 601.2c — “The player announces their choice of an appropriate object or player for each target the spell requires.”
    CR 601.2h — “The player pays the total cost.”
  5. Best line: spend Yawgmoth's SECOND ability now. Activate '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate' — tap two Swamps and discard the card you just drew. Grizzly Bears is the only permanent or player in the game with a counter on it, so it is the only legal proliferate choice, and it is chosen on resolution, not targeted.
    Grizzly Bears gets a second -1/-1 counter and becomes 0/0. This is the non-obvious half of the Nest interaction, so state it in full: the definition of proliferate says what is done but never names who does it, and the actor comes from resolution instead — the controller of the ability follows its instructions, and you control this proliferate ability, so YOU are the player who puts that counter on. That is exactly Nest of Scarabs' trigger condition ('Whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature'), so Nest of Scarabs triggers. State-based actions then bury Grizzly Bears, and Blood Artist triggers on its death (Blood Artist reads 'this creature or another creature dies' with no controller restriction, so an opponent's creature counts). Both triggers are yours and you order them. Resolve: Insect token D is created; DRAIN 2 — opponent B loses 1 life (40 -> 39), you gain 1 (40 -> 41). Your Insects: B, C, D. Mana left: one untapped Swamp. Hand: 0. Library: 59. The point of this activation is that its cost is mana and a card, NOT a creature: it removes the Bears and still makes an Insect without spending a body.
    CR 701.34a — “To proliferate means to choose any number of permanents and/or players that have a counter, then give each one additional counter of each kind that permanent or player already has.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 603.3b — “If multiple abilities have triggered since the last time a player received priority, the abilities are placed on the stack in a two-part process.”
  6. ACTIVATION 2 of the sacrifice ability: pay 1 life (41 -> 40), sacrifice Insect B, target Insect C. (Targeting the very Insect you are sacrificing would be legal to announce, but at resolution that target is no longer where it was when targeted, so the ability would not resolve at all — which is why this mode needs two Insects on the battlefield.)
    Blood Artist triggers on Insect B's death and goes on the stack above Yawgmoth's ability; it resolves first — DRAIN 3, opponent C 40 -> 39, you 40 -> 41. Yawgmoth's ability then resolves: -1/-1 counter on Insect C (1/1 -> 0/0), draw a card (library 58, hand 1). Nest triggers. State-based actions bury Insect C for toughness 0, and Blood Artist triggers again — DRAIN 4, opponent D 40 -> 39, you 41 -> 42. Nest's trigger resolves: Insect token E. Net for the activation: two drains, one card drawn, and your Insect count fell from 3 to 2 (D and E).
    CR 608.2b — “A target that’s no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted is illegal.”
  7. ACTIVATION 3: pay 1 life (42 -> 41), sacrifice Insect D, target Insect E. Same shape as activation 2.
    DRAIN 5 (Insect D dies): opponent A 39 -> 38, you 41 -> 42. The counter makes Insect E 0/0 and state-based actions bury it; DRAIN 6: opponent B 39 -> 38, you 42 -> 43. Draw (library 57, hand 2). Nest makes Insect token F. Your Insect count: 1.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  8. ACTIVATION 4 in this line — the one that consumes the payoff. With a single Insect left there is no second Insect to target, and Yawgmoth cannot be targeted by his own ability: he is a Human Cleric permanent, so his ability is an ability from a source with the stated quality and his protection from Humans forbids it. The only remaining legal creature target is Blood Artist itself. Pay 1 life (43 -> 42), sacrifice Insect F, target Blood Artist.
    DRAIN 7 (Insect F dies): opponent C 39 -> 38, you 42 -> 43. Yawgmoth's ability resolves: a -1/-1 counter makes Blood Artist -1/0. Draw (library 56, hand 3). Nest triggers. State-based actions bury Blood Artist for toughness 0. Blood Artist's ability is a leaves-the-battlefield ability, and leaves-the-battlefield abilities are among the zone-change triggers that LOOK BACK IN TIME. The mechanism is the general rule those exceptions hang off: for such an ability the game looks back in time to determine if it triggers, using the existence of those abilities and the appearance of objects immediately prior to the event. So Blood Artist sees its own death: DRAIN 8, opponent D 39 -> 38, you 43 -> 44. Nest's trigger resolves into Insect token G. Blood Artist is now in the graveyard and NO further death in this line drains anyone.
    CR 702.16b — “A permanent or player with protection can’t be targeted by spells with the stated quality and can’t be targeted by abilities from a source with the stated quality.”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 603.6c — “Leaves-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent moves from the battlefield to another zone, or when a phased-in permanent leaves the game because its owner leaves the game.”
    CR 603.10a — “Some zone-change triggers look back in time. These are leaves-the-battlefield abilities”
    CR 603.10 — “the game “looks back in time” to determine if those abilities trigger, using the existence of those abilities and the appearance of objects immediately prior to the event”
  9. ANSWER (a), part 1 — separate what the BOARD fixes from what the LINE chooses.
    BOARD-LEVEL INVARIANT (the answer to 'how far does this board go'): a maximum of EIGHT creature deaths occur while Blood Artist is on the battlefield, hence EIGHT Blood Artist triggers and EIGHT life removed. That number is a property of the board and every optimal line reaches it. THE ACTIVATION COUNT IS LINE-DEPENDENT and is not a board property. Line 1, the one resolved in steps 1-8: four draining sacrifice activations plus one proliferate, after which a fifth sacrifice activation (sacrifice Insect G, zero targets — 'up to one target creature' permits zero, so the ability is not targeted at all) is legal but drains nobody because Blood Artist is already dead; that leaves Yawgmoth as your only creature and 'Sacrifice another creature' can never be paid again. Line 2, equally optimal: at step 8 instead sacrifice Insect F choosing ZERO targets (DRAIN 7; Blood Artist survives, no counter and so no Nest token), then as a fifth activation sacrifice BLOOD ARTIST itself with zero targets — its own leaves-the-battlefield trigger sees its death, DRAIN 8. That line runs FIVE draining sacrifice activations for the same eight drains, and ends at 43 life, library 55, with no Insect token G. So: 8 drains is the board's number; '4 draining activations' belongs to line 1 and '5' to line 2. ALTERNATIVES CHECKED AND REJECTED. (i) Skipping proliferate yields SEVEN drains, and the reason previously given — that killing the Bears with an activation 'costs a body' — is WITHDRAWN as false: that activation is body-NEUTRAL for you (sacrifice one Insect, Nest returns one, and the Bears was never yours). The true reason is that proliferate removes the Bears while spending no creature at all, so you enter the rest of the line one body richer, and on this board the remaining reach from any state is (drains so far) + 2 x (your Insects), because each Insect converts into its own death plus the death of whatever your counter kills. Proliferate branch: 2 + 2x3 = 8. Activation branch: 3 + 2x2 = 7. The extra body is worth 2 and the forgone activation only bought 1. (ii) Zero-target activations taken mid-line yield 1 drain instead of 2 for the same body, so they never beat targeting an Insect while a second Insect exists. (iii) Targeting the very creature you are sacrificing: the sacrifice puts it into the graveyard, so by the time the ability resolves it is no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted and the only target is illegal, so the ability does not resolve (and being a token it has by then ceased to exist as well — 704.5d, cited at step 1 — which is stronger than the illegality needs), costing you the counter, the Nest token AND the card draw, for one drain instead of two.
    CR 115.6 — “A spell or ability that requires targets may allow zero targets to be chosen. Such a spell or ability is still said to require targets, but that spell or ability is targeted only if one or more targets have been chosen for it.”
    CR 608.2b — “If all its targets, for every instance of the word “target,” are now illegal, the spell or ability doesn’t resolve.”
  10. ANSWER (a), part 2 — what ends it. Test each candidate bound named in the question.
    NOT your library: line 1 draws 4 cards and 56 of 60 remain (line 2 draws 5 and leaves 55), so the deck-out state-based action is never approached. NOT your life: you pay 1 life per sacrifice activation and gain 1 life per drain, 4 paid against 8 gained, so you finish at 44 — HIGHER than you started. Life is a net gain and can never be the constraint while Blood Artist lives. NOT opposing toughness as a grind: Grizzly Bears' toughness of 2 supplies exactly ONE survivable counter, and that window closes in step 3. What ends the engine is RUNNING OUT OF BODIES AND LEGAL TARGETS, by arithmetic: once Grizzly Bears is dead, every remaining creature target (a 1/1 Insect, the 0/1 Blood Artist) dies to a single counter, so such an activation removes two of your creatures while Nest returns one — net minus one. (Separately and truly: state-based actions bury the target before Nest's trigger is put on the stack, which is why the replacement Insect always arrives after the death it replaces and can never be that activation's target. That is a targeting fact, not the source of the minus one.) Yawgmoth's protection from Humans forbids him as a target and 'another creature' forbids him as the sacrifice, so the last legal target is Blood Artist and the engine's final act is eating its own payoff. This is a strictly finite chain, not a loop with no way to stop, so no draw question arises.
    CR 704.5b — “If a player attempted to draw a card from a library with no cards in it since the last time state-based actions were checked, that player loses the game.”
    CR 704.3 — “If any state-based actions are performed as a result of a check, the check is repeated; otherwise all triggered abilities that are waiting to be put on the stack are put on the stack, then the check is repeated.”
  11. ANSWER (b) — total reach across the pod, and the straight yes/no.
    EIGHT life total is removed from the opposing side, out of 160. Blood Artist's ability says 'target player loses 1 life' — one instance of the word target, singular — and a triggered ability's targets are declared when it is put on the stack, by the same announcement process a spell uses. Each of the eight triggers is therefore a SEPARATE ability object making its own single choice, so the eight may be spread or all aimed at one player, but no single trigger can ever reach more than one. Eight creature deaths occurred while Blood Artist was on the battlefield (Insect A, Grizzly Bears, Insect B, Insect C, Insect D, Insect E, Insect F, Blood Artist itself), so eight triggers and eight points of life loss. Spread evenly the pilot takes 2 from each opponent: 38 / 38 / 38 / 38. Concentrated on one player it takes that player from 40 to 32 and leaves the other three at 40. NO — this line cannot reduce all four opponents from 40 to 0. It falls short by 152, and the requirement is not a few more iterations but 160 creature deaths with Blood Artist on the battlefield against the 8 this board produces. The single-target wording is doing a fraction of the work of 'each opponent loses 1 life', but the substitute figure is NOT one number, and the reason is BODIES rather than the Bears. This board holds three creature bodies: Blood Artist, Insect A (already sacrificed to pay the cost) and Insect B. Both substitutes read 'creature you control' — Zulaport Cutthroat 'this creature or another creature you control dies', Bastion of Remembrance 'a creature you control dies' — so both miss Grizzly Bears, opponent A's creature, identically; that is the same one death for each and it is not what separates them. ZULAPORT CUTTHROAT IS ITSELF A CREATURE. Substituted into Blood Artist's slot it preserves all three bodies, so the terminal activation still has a legal creature target (Zulaport itself), Zulaport dies as the engine's final act and its own leaves-the-battlefield trigger sees that death: SEVEN deaths of creatures you control, the seventh being the drainer itself, and 7 x 4 = 28 life removed. Penalty against Blood Artist's 8: 3.5x. BASTION OF REMEMBRANCE IS AN ENCHANTMENT AND NEVER DIES. Substituted into that same slot it REMOVES a body and leaves two, so the terminal activation has no legal creature target at all — Yawgmoth is unreachable through protection from Humans — it takes zero targets, Bastion is never on the receiving end of anything, and only Insects A through F die: SIX deaths of creatures you control, and 6 x 4 = 24 life removed. Penalty: 3.0x. SISTER-STACK RECONCILIATION, stated LIKE-FOR-LIKE so this does not read as self-contradiction. Both substitution figures above are computed on a WITH-proliferate line (the proliferate of step 5), so each may only be set against a with-proliferate figure. Stack 003's board is identical to this one but for the drainer slot and it also runs the proliferate, so its 28 for Zulaport IS the 28 reached here — like-for-like, and it stands. Stack 002's headline 28 is a DIFFERENT QUANTITY: it is 002's NO-proliferate board invariant, and 002 carries a separate proliferate_variant reporting 8 deaths of creatures you control, 32 life removed, each opponent at 32 and you at 43, with the explicit instruction that the two are never blended. So the like-for-like Bastion comparison is this board's 24 against 002's 32, NOT against 002's 28. The whole of that 8-life gap is ONE BODY: 002's board carries a 1/1 white Human Soldier token — the very token Bastion's own enters-the-battlefield ability creates — which this board does not, so 002 runs its with-proliferate line from three creature bodies (Insect A, Insect B, the Soldier) where this board has two (Insect A, Insect B). Under step 9's own rule an extra body on this engine is worth TWO deaths, its own plus the death of whatever its counter kills, so the delta is +2 deaths and +2 x 4 = +8 life: 6 deaths / 24 life here, 8 deaths / 32 life there. The +2 holds exactly even though a Bastion line's TERMINAL body yields only one death (with no creature drainer to receive the last counter, the final activation takes zero targets), because both boards pay that same terminal penalty once. It is a two-death delta, not the one-death 'restored seventh death' asserted in the previous iteration, which is withdrawn as false. Neither figure is a correction to 002. Without the proliferate the Blood Artist figure is 7, distributed 2 / 2 / 2 / 1, and the answer is the same NO.
    CR 603.3d — “The remainder of the process for putting a triggered ability on the stack is identical to the process for casting a spell listed in rules 601.2c–d.”
    CR 115.1 — “These targets are declared as part of the process of putting the spell or ability on the stack.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
After the line
Your life
44
Your battlefield
Yawgmoth, Thran Physician (2/4, no counters), Nest of Scarabs (enchantment), Insect token G (1/1 black Insect, no counters), Swamp (untapped), Swamp (tapped), Swamp (tapped)
Opponents
Opponent A: 38, Opponent B: 38, Opponent C: 38, Opponent D: 38
