Case A-005 Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs + South Wind Avatar: a lifegain-triggered drain, and a 4-toughness body
Rules version June 19, 2026 · status: cleared in 4 review cycle(s) · tap to open Verified
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then run the engine as far as the rules allow. (a) South Wind Avatar reads 'Whenever another creature you control dies, you gain life equal to its toughness' and 'Whenever you gain life, each opponent loses 1 life'. A dying 1/1 Insect has toughness 1 at the moment it dies only if it carries no -1/-1 counter — state what its toughness actually is when it dies here, and therefore whether the lifegain trigger fires at all. Give the exact number of completed activations and what ends the loop. (b) South Wind Avatar is a 3/4 — the only body on this board that can absorb more than one -1/-1 counter. Does that change the bound, and by how much?
  1. Account for the cost that has already been paid. The scenario's board list annotates Insect token A as "already sacrificed to pay the cost of the ability now on the stack", so A is NOT on the battlefield as this resolution begins: Yawgmoth's ability was activated by paying 1 life (you 40 -> 39) and sacrificing Insect token A. Sacrificing A moved it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard, so A died, and South Wind Avatar's first ability ('Whenever another creature you control dies, you gain life equal to its toughness') triggered on that cost payment. Stated explicitly so nothing is left to inference: every number below is computed from that reading of the board, which is the same house convention this deck's stack 001 uses for a sacrifice paid as a cost.
    The Avatar's death trigger is waiting to be put on the stack. It goes on the stack the next time a player would receive priority - which is immediately after activation finishes - so it sits ABOVE Yawgmoth's activated ability and will resolve FIRST. The scenario's stack listing (one object) is therefore incomplete: the true stack is [pos 0: Yawgmoth's ability, pos 1: Avatar lifegain trigger].
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 700.4 — “The term dies means “is put into a graveyard from the battlefield.””
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority.”
  2. Fix the targets and the permanent targeting constraint. Yawgmoth's ability reads 'Put a -1/-1 counter on up to one target creature and draw a card.' Its target (Grizzly Bears) was locked in as the ability was activated, not on resolution - and 115.1c points at 602.2b, which makes the whole 601.2b-i announcement sequence apply to activating an ability. Yawgmoth himself has protection from Humans and is the source of a Human's ability, so he is never a legal target for it.
    Grizzly Bears is the target for this activation. For every later activation, the legal target pool is: Grizzly Bears (while alive), South Wind Avatar (while alive), and Insect tokens on the battlefield at the moment targets are announced - never Yawgmoth. Nest of Scarabs is an enchantment, not a creature, so it is never a target either. The supply of creatures that can absorb a counter without costing you a body is what ultimately ends the engine.
    CR 115.1c — “The target(s) are chosen as the ability is activated; see rule 602.2b.”
    CR 702.16b — “A permanent or player with protection can’t be targeted by spells with the stated quality and can’t be targeted by abilities from a source with the stated quality.”
  3. Resolve the top object: South Wind Avatar's lifegain trigger for Insect token A. This is a leaves-the-battlefield/dies trigger, one of the trigger classes that looks back in time, and 'its toughness' is information about an object no longer on the battlefield, so the value comes from last known information. Insect A carried NO -1/-1 counter - it was sacrificed as a cost, not killed by a counter - so its last known toughness on the battlefield was 1.
    You gain 1 life. Life: you 39 -> 40.
    CR 603.6c — “Leaves-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent moves from the battlefield to another zone, or when a phased-in permanent leaves the game because its owner leaves the game.”
    CR 603.10a — “Some zone-change triggers look back in time. These are leaves-the-battlefield abilities, abilities that trigger when a player sacrifices a permanent, abilities that trigger when a card leaves a graveyard, and abilities that trigger when an object that all players can see is put into a hand or library.”
    CR 608.2h — “If the effect requires information from a specific object, including the source of the ability itself, the effect uses the current information of that object if it’s in the public zone it was expected to be in; if it’s no longer in that zone, or if the effect has moved it from a public zone to a hidden zone, the effect uses the object’s last known information.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  4. The life gain is a real (nonzero) life gain event, so South Wind Avatar's second ability ('Whenever you gain life, each opponent loses 1 life') triggers. Cited here rather than assumed: an ability written 'whenever you gain life' is treated as though it read 'whenever a source causes you to gain life', so it keys on the EVENT and fires once per gain, whatever the size of the gain. It goes on the stack and resolves.
    Each of the four opponents loses 1 life. Opponents: 40 -> 39 each. Four life lost in total from this one drain - 1 per opponent per lifegain event, never 1 per point of life gained.
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
    CR 119.9 — “Some triggered abilities are written, “Whenever [a player] gains life, . . . .” Such abilities are treated as though they are written, “Whenever a source causes [a player] to gain life, . . . .””
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  5. Resolve Yawgmoth's activated ability: put a -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears and draw a card. The counter subtracts from power and toughness, making Grizzly Bears 1/1. Nest of Scarabs reads 'Whenever YOU put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature, create that many 1/1 black Insect creature tokens', and that condition has two halves, both of which must be shown. (i) A counter is put on a creature: Grizzly Bears is a creature, and the trigger is unrestricted as to whose creature it is, so an opponent's creature counts. (ii) YOU put it: the counter is placed by a resolving activated ability you control, and it is the controller of an ability who follows its instructions, so you are the player putting the counter. Both halves are met, so Nest triggers (trigger matching per CR 603.2, cited at step 4). Then resolve that Nest of Scarabs trigger, the next object on the stack: create one 1/1 black Insect creature token (call it C).
    Grizzly Bears is 1/1 with one -1/-1 counter. You draw a card (library 60 -> 59). Nest of Scarabs triggered once, for one counter, and that trigger resolves into Insect C. ACTIVATION 1 COMPLETE: you control Yawgmoth, Nest of Scarabs, South Wind Avatar (3/4), Insect B and Insect C. Body count is NET NEUTRAL - one Insect was consumed as a cost and one was created by the counter - and life is net neutral too (paid 1, gained 1). This is why the engine is a loop rather than a one-shot, and why neither life nor the library is what bounds it. The bound is the supply of -1/-1 counter targets that can absorb a counter without costing you a body.
    CR 122.1a — “A +X/+Y counter on a creature or on a creature card in a zone other than the battlefield, where X and Y are numbers, adds X to that object’s power and Y to that object’s toughness. Similarly, -X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 111.1 — “Some effects put tokens onto the battlefield. A token is a marker used to represent any permanent that isn’t represented by a card.”
  6. ACTIVATION 2. Pay 1 life (you 39), sacrifice Insect B - it has no counters, so its last known toughness is 1: the Avatar gains you 1 life (you 40), which drains each opponent for 1 (opponents 38 each). The ability resolves putting the second -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears, making it 0/0. Before any player gets priority, state-based actions run first and put it in the graveyard; only then do triggered abilities go on the stack.
    Grizzly Bears dies. It is an OPPONENT'S creature, so South Wind Avatar's 'another creature YOU CONTROL dies' ability does not trigger for it - no life, no drain from its death. The Nest of Scarabs trigger from the counter still happened at the moment the counter was placed and is unaffected by the subsequent death: Insect D is created. Draw (library 58). Insects: C, D. Grizzly Bears has now absorbed its full 2 toughness worth of counters - 2 activations' worth of replacement Insects.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack (see rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities”).”
  7. THE CRUX - what an Insect's toughness actually is when a -1/-1 counter kills it. ACTIVATIONS 3, 4 and 5 each sacrifice a counterless Insect (the Avatar gains you 1, each opponent loses 1) and put counters 1, 2 and 3 on South Wind Avatar - a 3/4, and -1/-1 counters subtract from POWER as well as toughness (122.1a, cited at step 5), so it goes 2/3, then 1/2, then 0/1, never dying, because a 4-toughness body absorbs 3 counters and is still alive at 1 toughness - each counter making one replacement Insect. ACTIVATION 6 has THREE legal targets at announcement - South Wind Avatar, still alive at 0/1 with three counters, and BOTH Insects then on the battlefield, F and G. F counts precisely because targets are announced at 601.2c before the cost is paid at 601.2h (step 9): F is still on the battlefield when targets are chosen, so it is a legal target, even though paying the cost is about to sacrifice it. Targeting F is NOT self-defeating, and the reason step 9 gives for declining to target Insect J does not carry here. Step 9's argument works only because J is at that point the ONLY sacrificeable body, so targeting it forces the cost to sacrifice the very permanent it targeted. At activation 6 there are two counterless Insects, and the sacrifice is chosen at 601.2h - AFTER targets are announced at 601.2c (step 9) - so targeting F simply means you pay the cost with G instead. F and G are interchangeable counterless 1/1 tokens, so the two options are EQUIVALENT by F-G symmetry, not one good and one self-defeating. Activation 6's real choice is therefore binary: spend the counter on an Insect (F or G, identically) or on the Avatar, and it is decided on other grounds - the Avatar line is already costed below. The best line spends the counter on one of your own Insects, say G, sacrificing F. It is not compelled to. (The alternative is legal and instructive: spending the Avatar's fourth counter here still reaches 8 activations, but it retires the drain two activations early and yields only 6 drains, 24 opposing life instead of 28. That choice is where 4 points of drain live.) The targeted Insect becomes 0/0 and is put into the graveyard by the same state-based action that killed Grizzly Bears at step 6 (CR 704.5f), and - being a token - it then ceases to exist. The Avatar's first ability DOES trigger on that death and DOES resolve; but 'its toughness' is information about an object no longer on the battlefield, so it is read from last known information, and the Insect's last known toughness ON the battlefield was 0, not 1, because the counter was already on it. TRIGGER ORDERING, since two of your triggered abilities are waiting at that same moment: Nest of Scarabs (triggered mid-resolution, when the counter was placed) and the Avatar's dies-trigger (triggered during the state-based action check that killed the Insect). Neither has 'another ability triggering' as its trigger condition, so both go on the stack together in the first part of the 603.3b process, in whichever order you choose. The order is outcome-neutral here: the Nest trigger creates its token either way, and the Avatar's trigger gains 0 either way.
    You gain 0 life from that death. Gaining 0 life is not a life gain event, so South Wind Avatar's SECOND ability does not trigger at all: no opponent loses life for that Insect. Stated plainly - a counter-killed Insect is worth 0 life and 0 drain; only a SACRIFICED (counterless) Insect, last known toughness 1, is worth 1 life and 4 opposing life. Exactly one death in the optimal line is of the counter-killed kind. Running totals after activation 6: opponents 34 each, you 40, library 54, Insects: one (H). South Wind Avatar is 0/1 with three counters (a 3/4 reduced by three -1/-1 counters, power and toughness alike).
    CR 111.7 — “A token that’s in a zone other than the battlefield ceases to exist.”
    CR 608.2h — “If the effect requires information from a specific object, including the source of the ability itself, the effect uses the current information of that object if it’s in the public zone it was expected to be in; if it’s no longer in that zone, or if the effect has moved it from a public zone to a hidden zone, the effect uses the object’s last known information.”
    CR 119.9 — “If a player gains 0 life, no life gain event has occurred, and these abilities won’t trigger.”
    CR 603.3b — “First, each player, in APNAP order, puts each triggered ability they control with a trigger condition that isn’t another ability triggering on the stack in any order they choose.”
  8. ACTIVATION 7 - the Avatar's fourth toughness point, and its own death. Pay 1 life (you 39), sacrifice Insect H (counterless, toughness 1): the Avatar gains you 1 life (you 40) and each opponent loses 1 (opponents 33 each). Critically, the Avatar is still on the battlefield when that sacrifice happens - the cost is paid before the ability resolves - so this drain is collected in full. The ability then resolves, putting the FOURTH -1/-1 counter on South Wind Avatar, making it -1/0. State-based actions put it into the graveyard.
    South Wind Avatar dies. Its own ability reads 'Whenever ANOTHER creature you control dies', so its own death does not match its trigger event and it does not trigger for itself - no final life, no final drain. This is a permanent fact about the Avatar and it is load-bearing for the ceiling proved at step 11: the Avatar can never be its own drain. The Nest of Scarabs trigger from that fourth counter still resolves and makes Insect J - a body that is created AFTER the Avatar has already been put into the graveyard. Draw (library 53). The lifegain/drain engine is now off permanently: every later sacrifice gains nothing and drains nothing.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
  9. ACTIVATION 8 and the terminus, narrated in the order the rules actually run. Activating an ability follows the 601.2 sequence (step 2's 115.1c points at 602.2b for exactly this), and in that sequence targets are ANNOUNCED at 601.2c and the total cost is paid five lettered steps later at 601.2h, with 601.2d (dividing or distributing an effect), 601.2e (the legality check), 601.2f (determining the total cost) and 601.2g (activating mana abilities) all intervening. The only thing the argument needs is the DIRECTION - announcement precedes payment - and that direction is what 601.2c and 601.2h jointly fix. So at the moment targets are chosen for activation 8, Insect J is still on the battlefield and IS a legal target; Yawgmoth is not (step 2). Choosing zero targets is therefore a CHOICE the ability permits ('up to one target creature'), not a compulsion forced by an empty target pool. It is the correct choice: had J been targeted, paying the cost would then have sacrificed J, moving it off the battlefield (step 1's 701.21a), and on resolution the ability's only target would have been illegal - so the ability would not have resolved at all and you would have lost the card. With no target chosen, the cost is paid: 1 life (you 39) and Insect J sacrificed. No Avatar is on the battlefield, so no lifegain trigger and no drain. The ability resolves; no counter is placed, so Nest of Scarabs does not trigger and no replacement Insect is created; you draw a card (library 53 -> 52).
    LOOP ENDS. You now control only Yawgmoth, Nest of Scarabs and three Swamps - no other creature. The activation cost is 'Pay 1 life, Sacrifice another creature'; with no other creature on the battlefield, that cost is unpayable and cannot be paid, so there is no ninth activation. Note what the terminus rests on: the SACRIFICE COST becoming unpayable, not the target pool being empty. The engine is bounded by BODIES - not by life, which was net zero for as long as the Avatar lived and then cost 1 per activation out of a 40 life total.
    CR 601.2c — “The player announces their choice of an appropriate object or player for each target the spell requires.”
    CR 115.6 — “A spell or ability that requires targets may allow zero targets to be chosen.”
    CR 608.2b — “If the spell or ability specifies targets, it checks whether the targets are still legal. A target that’s no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted is illegal.”
    CR 608.2b — “If all its targets, for every instance of the word “target,” are now illegal, the spell or ability doesn’t resolve.”
    CR 601.2h — “Partial payments are not allowed. Unpayable costs can’t be paid.”
  10. DISPOSE OF PROLIFERATE - Yawgmoth's OTHER activated ability, which the scenario's mana line invites. Yawgmoth, Thran Physician also reads '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate.' The scenario grants {B}{B}{B} from three untapped Swamps, which the first ability never spends, and the hand starts empty but holds a card from activation 1's draw onward - so exactly ONE proliferate is available in this sequence (three Swamps cannot make a second {B}{B}). To proliferate is to choose any number of permanents and/or players that have a counter and give each one more counter of each kind it already has. What that can reach here: Grizzly Bears while it carries one counter, and South Wind Avatar once it carries counters. It can NEVER reach one of your Insects - a 1/1 Insect that takes a -1/-1 counter is 0/0 and is put into the graveyard by a state-based action before any player receives priority to activate anything (step 6's 704.5f), so no Insect ever bears a counter at a time when proliferate could be activated. No player here has poison or any other counter.
    It does NOT change either number. A proliferated -1/-1 counter is a counter YOU put on a creature, so Nest of Scarabs triggers and makes a token for free - no sacrifice, no life paid (step 5). But the token comes out of the SAME fixed pool the ceiling is computed from: the only creatures that can absorb a counter without costing you a body are Grizzly Bears and the Avatar, and their printed toughness caps that pool at 2 + 4 = 6 counters no matter who places them. A counter proliferate places is a counter no later activation can place, so it buys a body 1-for-1 out of capacity already counted at step 11. Activations stay at 8. Drains stay at 7 as well, because proliferate produces no death of a creature YOU control that gains nonzero life: it cannot kill an Insect (it can never target one), Grizzly Bears is an opponent's, and if it is used to finish the Avatar, the Avatar's death never triggers the Avatar's own ability (step 8). So: {B}{B}{B} on the board is real and the ability is genuinely activatable, and the answer to (a) and (b) is unchanged at 8 activations and 7 drains. (Sister stack 002, where proliferate DOES move the drain count from 7 to 8, differs precisely because that board's drain fires on your creatures dying at any toughness; here a counter-killed body gains 0 and drains nothing.)
    CR 701.34a — “To proliferate means to choose any number of permanents and/or players that have a counter, then give each one additional counter of each kind that permanent or player already has.”
  11. PROVE THE BOUND, by case exhaustion over how the Avatar leaves the battlefield - and answer (b). (i) THE BODY LEDGER. Every completed activation consumes exactly one creature other than Yawgmoth (the sacrifice) and returns exactly one Insect for each -1/-1 counter placed on a creature. A counter placed on one of YOUR Insects is body-neutral: +1 token, -1 Insect (step 7). A counter placed on Grizzly Bears or on the Avatar adds a body without removing one of yours - except the counter that finally kills the Avatar, which removes the Avatar itself as a sacrificeable body. Absorption is capped by printed toughness: Bears 2, Avatar 4. So the total number of creatures you can ever sacrifice is 3 (Insect A, Insect B, the Avatar) + (counters on Bears, at most 2) + (counters on the Avatar, at most 4) - (1 if the Avatar's counters killed it). Enumerate the cases: [A] the Avatar takes 4 counters and dies to them - 3 + 2 + 4 - 1 = 8; [B] the Avatar takes at most 3 counters and is SACRIFICED instead - 3 + 2 + 3 - 0 = 8; [C] the Avatar neither dies to a counter nor is sacrificed - it is then not a sacrificeable body at all, giving 2 + 2 + 3 = 7. No case exceeds 8, and steps 1-9 attain 8, so the bound is tight at 8. (This replaces the earlier claim that an eighth drain 'would require the Avatar alive at the eighth sacrifice, which caps activations at 7' - that reasoning is false, and case [B] is the counterexample: leave the Avatar on three counters and sacrifice it as activation 8 and you have 8 activations with the Avatar alive at the eighth sacrifice.) (ii) THE DRAIN CEILING IS 7, and the correct reason has two parts. A drain requires a nonzero lifegain event, which requires a creature you control to die with last known toughness at least 1 while the Avatar is on the battlefield; the only deaths that qualify are sacrifices of counterless Insects, because a counter-killed Insect dies at toughness 0 (step 7), Grizzly Bears is not yours (step 6), and the Avatar's own death never triggers its own 'another creature you control dies' ability (step 8). So drains are at most one per sacrifice, at most 8. That 8 is unreachable, and the proof is by SUPPLY OF BODIES - not by any claim that an 8-activation line sacrifices everything it has, which is FALSE: Insect G is consumed by a counter-kill and never sacrificed at all, which is the whole content of step 7. Suppose instead, for contradiction, that all 8 sacrifices drained. Each would then have to be the sacrifice of a counterless INSECT made while the Avatar was still on the battlefield - the Avatar itself can never be one of them, since its own death does not trigger its own 'another creature you control dies' ability (step 8). Now count how many Insect bodies can exist under that supposition. You start with 2 (A and B). Every further Insect is a Nest token, one per -1/-1 counter placed, and a counter placed on one of your own Insects is exactly body-neutral - it kills one and creates one (step 7) - so only counters placed on Grizzly Bears or on the Avatar can grow the supply. Bears absorbs at most 2. The Avatar can have absorbed at most 3 while still being on the battlefield, because a fourth counter kills it (step 8) and the supposition requires it alive at every one of the 8 sacrifices. That is at most 2 + 3 = 5 supply-growing counters, hence at most 5 tokens, hence at most 2 + 5 = 7 Insect bodies exist in time to be sacrificed - one short of the 8 the supposition demands. Contradiction, so at most 7 drains. The count is unchanged if proliferate places some of those counters (step 10), since they come out of the same fixed 2 + 4 absorption pool. The case split says the same thing concretely: in case [B] the Avatar leaves by being sacrificed and that sacrifice is its own death, which does not trigger it; in case [A] it is counter-killed, and the Nest token made by the killing counter is created after the Avatar is already in the graveyard (step 8) - a body that, being a token from a counter-kill rather than the last body standing, still has to be disposed of with no Avatar on the battlefield. Either way at least one of the 8 sacrifices is drainless, which is what the supply count proves in general - and the line in steps 1-9 attains 7, simultaneously with 8 activations.
    (b) YES, the 4-toughness body extends the bound, by exactly +4 activations. South Wind Avatar contributes 4 of the 6 non-Insect counter slots (+4), adds itself as a sacrificeable body (+1), and loses itself as a body to the very counter that kills it (-1): net +4. Without it, the ledger reads 2 starting Insects + 2 Grizzly Bears counters = 4 activations, and ZERO drains, since it is also the only source of lifegain on the board - so it supplies 100% of the damage. The library is not the constraint either: 8 draws against a stated 60-card library, where the rule that punishes an empty library would need roughly 60 activations against a hard ceiling of 8.
    CR 601.2h — “Partial payments are not allowed. Unpayable costs can’t be paid.”
    CR 104.3c — “If a player is required to draw more cards than are left in their library, they draw the remaining cards and then lose the game the next time a player would receive priority.”
  12. Tally. South Wind Avatar's first ability triggered 8 times over the run: seven sacrifices of counterless 1/1 Insects (last known toughness 1, so 1 life each) plus one counter-killed Insect at toughness 0 (0 life). Its second ability triggered 7 times, once per nonzero lifegain event, draining 1 from each of four opponents each time.
    (a) 8 completed activations, ended by having no creature other than Yawgmoth left to sacrifice. The Avatar's drain DOES fire - 7 times, not 8 and not 10 - for 7 x 4 = 28 total opposing life. Each opponent goes 40 -> 33; 160 opposing life becomes 132. You end at 39 (40 - 8 paid + 7 gained), having drawn 8 cards (library 52). This is decisively NOT infinite and not near-infinite: the deaths that would have to be free are the counter-killed Insects, and those gain 0 life and drain nothing. Commander Spellbook's 'Near-infinite' tag is wrong for this board.
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
After the line
Your life
39
Your battlefield
Yawgmoth, Thran Physician (2/4, no counters, untapped), Nest of Scarabs, Swamp (untapped), Swamp (untapped), Swamp (untapped)
Opponents
Opponent A: 33, Opponent B: 33, Opponent C: 33, Opponent D: 33

Case A-006 Persist + Nest of Scarabs: does a creature that ENTERS with a -1/-1 counter trigger the Nest?
Rules version June 19, 2026 · status: cleared in 2 review cycle(s) · tap to open Verified
The question put to the record
Persist resolves and returns Fume Spitter to the battlefield with a -1/-1 counter on it. Does Nest of Scarabs trigger and create an Insect token? Cite the rule that decides whether a permanent entering the battlefield with counters counts as a player putting those counters on it, and state explicitly whether the answer would differ if an effect instead placed a counter on a creature already on the battlefield.
  1. Persist resolves. Its one target is rechecked for legality first: Fume Spitter is still a nonlegendary creature card in your graveyard, so the target is legal and the spell resolves. You follow its instruction in the order written: return Fume Spitter from your graveyard to the battlefield with a -1/-1 counter on it. Persist does not name a controller, so Fume Spitter enters under YOUR control (you are the player the effect instructed to put it onto the battlefield). Persist itself is then put into your graveyard as the final part of its resolution.
    Fume Spitter is on the battlefield under your control with one -1/-1 counter on it. Persist is in your graveyard. Nothing has been targeted or damaged; life totals are unchanged at 40 apiece.
    CR 608.2b — “If the spell or ability specifies targets, it checks whether the targets are still legal.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 110.2a — “If an effect instructs a player to put an object onto the battlefield, that object enters the battlefield under that player’s control unless the effect states otherwise.”
    CR 608.2n — “As the final part of an instant or sorcery spell’s resolution, the spell is put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  2. THE RULING, and it is decided by one rule: 122.6. Nest of Scarabs asks 'Whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature.' Rule 122.6 says that a spell or ability referring to counters being put on an object refers BOTH to putting counters on it while it is on the battlefield AND to an object that is given counters as it enters the battlefield. So 'entering with a -1/-1 counter' is not a separate, exempt category — it is inside the reference. The second half of the question, WHO put it, is answered by 122.6a: the effect may specify a player, and Persist does not, so the object's controller puts those counters on it. Fume Spitter's controller is you (step 1). Therefore YOU put one -1/-1 counter on a creature, and Nest of Scarabs' trigger condition is satisfied. ANSWER: YES, Nest of Scarabs triggers.
    The trigger condition of Nest of Scarabs is met. Note the citation this step deliberately does NOT lean on: sibling stacks 001-005 reach 'you are the actor' through 608.2c, because there the counter is placed by an ability you control following its instructions on a creature already on the battlefield. That reasoning does not by itself reach a permanent entering with counters — no instruction is being followed against an object on the battlefield at that moment. It is 122.6 that brings the entering case inside the phrase, and 122.6a that names you rather than 608.2c. Both roads arrive at the same place here, but only 122.6/122.6a is load-bearing for THIS scenario.
    CR 122.6 — “This refers to putting counters on that object while it’s on the battlefield and also to an object that’s given counters as it enters the battlefield.”
    CR 122.6a — “the effect causing the object to be given counters may specify which player puts those counters on it”
    CR 122.6a — “If the effect doesn’t specify a player, the object’s controller puts those counters on it.”
    CR 110.2a — “If an effect instructs a player to put an object onto the battlefield, that object enters the battlefield under that player’s control unless the effect states otherwise.”
  3. The trigger fires. The game event (you putting one -1/-1 counter on a creature) matches Nest of Scarabs' trigger condition, so the ability triggers automatically at that moment. It does nothing yet and is not yet on the stack.
    One Nest of Scarabs trigger is waiting to be put on the stack. 'That many' is locked to the number of -1/-1 counters in the event: ONE.
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers. The ability doesn’t do anything at this point.”
  4. State-based actions are checked BEFORE the waiting trigger goes on the stack. Fume Spitter is a 1/1 with one -1/-1 counter on it, which subtracts from power and toughness, making it 0/0. A creature with toughness 0 or less is put into its owner's graveyard — Fume Spitter dies immediately, without ever having been available to sacrifice. The check is then repeated; nothing more is performed, and the Nest of Scarabs trigger is put on the stack. Fume Spitter dying does NOT undo the trigger: it had already triggered in step 3, and the trigger is an independent object on the stack that no longer depends on its source event or on Fume Spitter.
    Fume Spitter is in your graveyard. Your battlefield: Nest of Scarabs, three untapped Swamps. Stack: one Nest of Scarabs trigger. This is the pilot's real lesson — Persist on a 1-toughness creature returns it and kills it in the same breath, and what you actually keep is the Insect.
    CR 122.1a — “Similarly, -X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack (see rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities”).”
    CR 704.3 — “If any state-based actions are performed as a result of a check, the check is repeated; otherwise all triggered abilities that are waiting to be put on the stack are put on the stack, then the check is repeated.”
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority.”
  5. You receive priority and pass; every opponent passes in succession (the scenario's stated assumption), so the Nest of Scarabs trigger resolves. It creates that many — one — 1/1 black Insect creature tokens: put one token with those characteristics onto the battlefield under your control. The ability then leaves the stack.
    One 1/1 black Insect creature token enters the battlefield under your control. State-based actions are checked again and none apply (the Insect is 1/1, nothing else is on the battlefield to check). You receive priority in your precombat main phase with the stack empty.
    CR 117.4 — “If all players pass in succession (that is, if all players pass without taking any actions in between passing), the spell or ability on top of the stack resolves”
    CR 701.7a — “To create one or more tokens with certain characteristics, put the specified number of tokens with the specified characteristics onto the battlefield.”
    CR 111.1 — “Some effects put tokens onto the battlefield. A token is a marker used to represent any permanent that isn’t represented by a card.”
    CR 608.2n — “As the final part of an ability’s resolution, the ability is removed from the stack and ceases to exist.”
  6. THE CONTRAST THE QUESTION ASKS FOR: would the answer differ if an effect instead placed a -1/-1 counter on a creature ALREADY on the battlefield? No. The answer is YES in both cases, and 122.6 is precisely the rule that makes them the same — it names both halves in one sentence. What changes is only which rule identifies YOU as the actor. For a creature already on the battlefield, an ability you control instructs you to put the counter and you follow that instruction (608.2c) — that is the route stacks 001-005 take. For a permanent entering with counters, no instruction is being followed against an on-battlefield object, so the actor comes from 122.6a's default: the object's controller. THE ONE PLACE THE TWO GENUINELY DIVERGE, and a pilot should know it: because 122.6a hands the counters to the object's CONTROLLER, a nonlegendary creature returned to the battlefield under an OPPONENT's control with a -1/-1 counter would have that counter put on by that opponent, not by you, and Nest of Scarabs — which says 'whenever YOU put' — would not trigger. In this scenario Fume Spitter enters under your control, so that caveat does not bite.
    Ruling for the deck: Persist IS a legitimate ignition source for Nest of Scarabs — the -1/-1 counter it brings along counts as you putting a counter on a creature, and the Nest pays out one Insect. The goldfish_targets.json listing of Persist as an ignition source is NOT overstated on the rules. The honest caveat is a different one and it is about the BODY, not the trigger: Persist returning a 1-toughness creature (Fume Spitter) yields the Insect and immediately loses the creature to state-based actions, so it ignites without leaving a sacrifice body behind.
    CR 122.6 — “Some spells and abilities refer to counters being put on an object. This refers to putting counters on that object while it’s on the battlefield and also to an object that’s given counters as it enters the battlefield.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 122.6a — “If the effect doesn’t specify a player, the object’s controller puts those counters on it.”
After the line
Your life
40
Your battlefield
Nest of Scarabs (enchantment), Swamp (untapped), Swamp (untapped), Swamp (untapped), 1/1 black Insect creature token (created by Nest of Scarabs)
Opponents
Opponent A: 40, Opponent B: 40, Opponent C: 40, Opponent D: 40

↩ Back to this line in The Kill · ↑ Contents

Case A-008 Mirkwood Bats + Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs: does one activation pay the drain twice?
Rules version June 19, 2026 · status: cleared in 5 review cycle(s) · tap to open Verified
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then run the engine as far as the rules allow. (a) How many Mirkwood Bats triggers does ONE Yawgmoth activation paid with an Insect token produce, and why? (b) Over the whole engine, how many Bats triggers occur in total and how much life does each opponent lose — and state whether that is more than the 28 that stacks 002 and 003 measured on this same board with a death-metering drainer.
  1. Fix the reading and restore the trigger the stack picture omits. THE READING: the scenario's board annotates 'Insect token A' as ALREADY SACRIFICED to pay the cost of the ability now on the stack, so the pilot enters this resolution holding exactly one live Insect (token B) plus Mirkwood Bats (2/3) and Yawgmoth (2/4). THE OMISSION: sacrificing Insect A was a sacrifice of a token while Mirkwood Bats was on the battlefield, so Bats has already triggered and that trigger is not shown. Establish precisely when it went on the stack, because the whole question turns on the two halves of Bats' condition arriving at different times. Activating Yawgmoth's ability put the ability itself on the stack FIRST (602.2a) and the activation cost -- 1 life and 'Sacrifice another creature' -- was paid LAST, as the final step of the activation process (602.2b routing to 601.2h). So Insect A was sacrificed (701.21a) while Yawgmoth's ability was already the topmost object on the stack. Nothing happens at the moment an ability triggers; the Bats trigger waits and is put on the stack the next time a player would receive priority (117.2a), which is immediately after the activation completes, and it becomes the topmost object (603.3) -- i.e. ABOVE Yawgmoth's ability.
    Stack (top-down): Mirkwood Bats' trigger from Insect A's sacrifice; Yawgmoth's -1/-1 ability targeting Grizzly Bears. You are at 39 (1 life already paid); each opponent at 40. Bats trigger count: 1. This is the sacrifice half of Bats' condition and it is already banked BEFORE Yawgmoth's ability has done anything at all.
    CR 602.2a — “It becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
    CR 602.2b — “The remainder of the process for activating an ability is identical to the process for casting a spell”
    CR 601.2h — “The player pays the total cost.”
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 117.2a — “Each time a player would receive priority, each ability that has triggered but hasn’t yet been put on the stack is put on the stack.”
    CR 603.3 — “The ability becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
  2. Bats trigger #1 resolves: 'each opponent loses 1 life.' Four opponents, four separate life losses. Bats grants YOU no life -- unlike the death-metering drainer of stack 003, which gained you 1 per drain.
    Opponents A/B/C/D: 40 -> 39 each. You remain at 39. Bats triggers so far: 1. Table life removed: 4.
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  3. Yawgmoth's activated ability resolves. Its target is rechecked first: Grizzly Bears is still on the battlefield and unchanged, so it is still legal (608.2b). Put a -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears and draw a card. The counter subtracts from power and toughness (122.1a), so Bears is 1/1.
    Grizzly Bears: 2/2 -> 1/1 with one -1/-1 counter, toughness above 0, survives. Library 60 -> 59, hand 1. Putting that counter triggers Nest of Scarabs ('Whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature, create that many 1/1 black Insect creature tokens'). Mirkwood Bats does NOT trigger on the counter -- Bats meters creation and sacrifice of tokens, not counters and not deaths.
    CR 608.2b — “If the spell or ability specifies targets, it checks whether the targets are still legal.”
    CR 122.1a — “-X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
  4. ANSWER (a). State-based actions find nothing (Bears is 1/1), the Nest of Scarabs trigger is put on the stack, all players pass in succession per the scenario's stated assumption (117.4), and it resolves: exactly ONE -1/-1 counter was placed, so Nest creates 'that many' = one 1/1 black Insect token (701.7a). Creating it is the OTHER half of Bats' condition, so Bats triggers a second time; that trigger then resolves for another 1 life from each opponent. On the singular wording: Bats reads 'a token', and 603.2c settles both directions -- an ability triggers only once each time its trigger event occurs, but repeatedly if ONE event contains multiple occurrences. So a Nest trigger that placed N counters at once and created N tokens in a single event would trigger Bats N times, once per token; every Nest trigger in this line places exactly one counter and creates exactly one token, so each is worth exactly one Bats trigger. THEREFORE: one Yawgmoth activation paid with an Insect token and placing a counter produces TWO Bats triggers -- one for the sacrifice, one for the creation -- and they are NOT simultaneous and never sit on the stack together. The sacrifice trigger was on the stack ABOVE the activated ability and resolved before it (step 1); the creation trigger did not exist until Nest's own trigger resolved, two resolutions later. An activation paid with a token that places no counter is worth exactly ONE (the sacrifice half only).
    Insect token C is created. Opponents 39 -> 38 each. Bats triggers so far: 2 -- and the first Yawgmoth activation is complete, so the answer to (a) is TWO. Your board: Yawgmoth 2/4, Nest of Scarabs, Mirkwood Bats 2/3, Insect B, Insect C. Bears 1/1. Table life removed: 8.
    CR 117.4 — “If all players pass in succession (that is, if all players pass without taking any actions in between passing), the spell or ability on top of the stack resolves”
    CR 701.7a — “To create one or more tokens with certain characteristics, put the specified number of tokens with the specified characteristics onto the battlefield.”
    CR 603.2c — “An ability triggers only once each time its trigger event occurs. However, it can trigger repeatedly if one event contains multiple occurrences.”
  5. The precision that separates this drainer from stacks 002 and 003: a counter-KILLED Insect pays Bats NOTHING. When a -1/-1 counter drops a 1/1 Insect to 0/0 it is put into its owner's graveyard as a state-based action (704.5f). That is a death, and 002/003's drainers meter exactly that. It is not a sacrifice: to sacrifice is for the permanent's CONTROLLER to move it to the graveyard (701.21a), an action a player takes to pay a cost or follow an instruction, whereas 704.5f is the game putting it there automatically. Bats' condition is 'create or sacrifice', so a counter-kill matches neither half. Only the creation half applies to those tokens -- they paid Bats once, when they were made.
    Established for the whole engine: every token is worth +1 Bats trigger when created, +1 more only if it is SACRIFICED (to Yawgmoth's cost), and 0 for dying to a counter. Bats is therefore not a strictly better Zulaport Cutthroat; it counts a different set of events.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
  6. Establish the engine's bound before running it, and answer the 2/3 question. Each activation spends one body (the sacrifice) and Nest returns at most one, so the loop is body-neutral only while a counter can land on a creature that is NOT one of your own Insects. The available counter sinks are: (i) Grizzly Bears, an opponent's creature -- two counter-placement events, the second killing it, and both are free to you because it is not your body; (ii) Mirkwood Bats itself. THIS IS WHERE THE 2/3 CHANGES THE BOUND. A 1/1 drainer (stack 003's) dies to its first counter and can never be a sink. Bats at 2/3 absorbs TWO -1/-1 counters and is 0/1 -- toughness 1, above 0, so 704.5f does not apply and it keeps draining (122.1a). Those two counters are two extra body-neutral counter events, each worth a free Nest token and therefore a free Bats trigger. A third counter would make Bats -1/0 and kill it, so two is the cap. (iii) Yawgmoth cannot be a sink at all: he is a Human Cleric and has protection from Humans, so his own ability -- an ability from a Human source -- can never target him (702.16b). And 'up to one target creature' permits choosing zero targets (115.6), so the pilot is never forced to put a counter anywhere.
    Counter-sink budget: Bears 2 events, Mirkwood Bats 2 events, then nothing but your own Insects (each such event kills a token and pays Bats nothing). The loop is strictly FINITE. Bats being a 2/3 rather than a 1/1 is worth +5 Bats triggers on this board, measured against the same Bats text on a 1/1 body: a 1/1 drainer dies to its first -1/-1 counter and can never be a counter sink, and that board tops out at 8 triggers where this one reaches 13. The +5 enumerates as +2 on the SACRIFICE half and +3 on the CREATION half, which is where it stops looking like a coincidence. THE +2 SACRIFICES, AND THE MECHANISM IS NOT WHAT IT LOOKS LIKE: Bats' first counter slot buys one body-neutral activation; its second slot makes the proliferate worth a second Nest token, which funds the other activation. Only the FIRST of Bats' two counters is a counter an activation aimed at it; the second arrives from the proliferate, which can only grow a permanent that ALREADY carries a counter (step 8), and no Insect of yours can ever carry a -1/-1 counter and live -- so that second counter never had an Insect alternative to be spared from. Nor does this line spare Insects at all: it counter-kills THREE of your own tokens (D, F and H, step 9) where the 1/1 board's best line kills two, and three is forced in every optimal line here, not a choice. What the 2/3 body buys is ACTIVATIONS, not bodies -- the engine therefore runs SIX Yawgmoth activations instead of four, and every activation pays the sacrifice half exactly once, because sacrificing a token IS this ability's cost. THE +3 CREATIONS: two of them are the Nest tokens those two extra activations make (five token-creating activations here against three, since the last activation in either line targets nothing and makes nothing), and the third is the proliferate -- Bats carrying counters gives proliferate a SECOND permanent to grow, so one proliferate yields two Nest triggers on this board against one on the 1/1 board. None of the +5 comes from the counter-killed tokens: those pay the creation half only and are never sacrificed (steps 5 and 9), so no token this line kills with a counter is counted twice.
    CR 702.16b — “A permanent or player with protection can’t be targeted by spells with the stated quality and can’t be targeted by abilities from a source with the stated quality.”
    CR 115.6 — “A spell or ability that requires targets may allow zero targets to be chosen.”
    CR 122.1a — “-X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
  7. Activation #2, spending the first of Bats' own two counter slots. Pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect B (a token) -- Bats triggers, and by step 1's sequencing that trigger goes on the stack above the ability and resolves first. Target: Mirkwood Bats itself (a legal target -- it has no protection or hexproof, and you may target your own creature). Bats trigger resolves; then the ability resolves, putting a -1/-1 counter on Bats (2/3 -> 1/2, alive) and drawing a card. SBAs are performed and only then is the resulting Nest trigger put on the stack (117.5); nothing dies. Nest resolves, creating Insect D, and Bats triggers on the creation.
    Two Bats triggers this activation, exactly as (a) says. Opponents 38 -> 36 each; you 39 -> 38 (1 life paid, no gain). Library 58, hand 2. Bats 1/2 with one -1/-1 counter. Bodies: Insect C, Insect D. Bats triggers so far: 4. Table life removed: 16.
    CR 117.5 — “then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  8. Now spend the three Swamps the scenario deliberately supplies -- Yawgmoth's -1/-1 ability costs no mana, so the only mana sink is his second ability: '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate.' Tap two Swamps, discard a card drawn earlier. Proliferate lets you CHOOSE any number of permanents that have a counter and give each one more of a kind already there (701.34a), so you choose exactly Grizzly Bears and Mirkwood Bats and nothing else. Bears goes to two counters (0/0); Bats goes to two counters (0/1, alive). This is one event that puts a -1/-1 counter on TWO different creatures, so Nest of Scarabs -- 'whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on A CREATURE' -- triggers twice, once per creature, by 603.2c's multiple-occurrences clause. SBAs run before those triggers go on the stack: Bears is 0/0 and is put into its OWNER's graveyard (704.5f); Bats at 0/1 survives. The two Nest triggers then resolve one at a time, each creating one token, each paying Bats once.
    Two tokens created (Insect E, Insect F) for two mana and a discard, with NO sacrifice spent -- two Bats triggers for free. Opponents 36 -> 34 each; you 38, hand 1, library 58. Grizzly Bears is dead and in opponent A's graveyard; its death pays Bats nothing (step 5). Bats 0/1 with two counters -- its sink capacity is now exhausted. Bodies: Insects C, D, E, F. One Swamp untapped, which can no longer buy anything (proliferate costs {B}{B}). Bats triggers so far: 6. Table life removed: 24.
    CR 701.34a — “To proliferate means to choose any number of permanents and/or players that have a counter, then give each one additional counter of each kind that permanent or player already has.”
    CR 603.2c — “it can trigger repeatedly if one event contains multiple occurrences”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  9. The terminal phase: every external sink is gone, so each remaining activation must aim its counter at one of your own Insects (or at nothing). Aiming it at an Insect is strictly better than aiming at nothing -- the counter-kill itself pays Bats nothing (step 5), but it triggers Nest, and the token Nest makes IS a creation, worth one Bats trigger. So each of these activations is still worth TWO Bats triggers (sacrifice + creation) while costing a net one body. Run it three times. Activation #3: pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect C (Bats trigger), target Insect D; D becomes 0/0, SBAs put it in the graveyard and it then ceases to exist, and only then does the Nest trigger go on the stack (117.5, 704.5f); Nest creates Insect G (Bats trigger). Activation #4: sacrifice Insect E, target Insect F, Nest creates Insect H -- identical shape. Activation #5: sacrifice Insect G, target Insect H, Nest creates Insect I -- identical shape.
    Six more Bats triggers across three activations. Opponents 34 -> 28 each; you 38 -> 35 (three more life paid). Library 58 -> 55, hand 4. Bodies fall 4 -> 3 -> 2 -> 1: only Insect I remains. Bats triggers so far: 12. Table life removed: 48. Note the shape: each of these activations kills two of your tokens and makes one, so the engine is now net -1 body per activation and is visibly winding down.
    CR 117.5 — “then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  10. Activation #6, the last one, and the two lines the pilot must decline. Pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect I -- a token, so Bats triggers one final time. For the counter, the only remaining creatures are Yawgmoth (untargetable by his own ability, step 6) and Mirkwood Bats at 0/1. Targeting Bats would be a THIRD counter on it: Bats becomes -1/0 and is put into the graveyard by state-based action before the resulting Nest trigger is ever put on the stack (704.5f), so the token that trigger creates would be created with no Bats on the battlefield and would pay nothing -- the option is worth exactly zero and costs the drainer. The pilot therefore chooses zero targets, which 'up to one target creature' expressly permits (115.6): no counter, no Nest trigger, no token. The engine also cannot continue past this: the only creatures left are Yawgmoth, who cannot pay his own cost ('Sacrifice ANOTHER creature'), and Bats, which is not a token -- sacrificing Bats would pay Bats nothing and remove the drainer, so it is declined too.
    One final Bats trigger. Opponents 28 -> 27 each; you 35 -> 34. Library 54, hand 5. No tokens remain. Bats triggers: 13. Table life removed: 52.
    CR 115.6 — “A spell or ability that requires targets may allow zero targets to be chosen.”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  11. ANSWER (b) and the final state-based-action check. Ledger of Bats triggers, by half. SACRIFICE half -- six tokens were sacrificed to Yawgmoth's cost: Insect A (pre-resolution, step 1), B, C, E, G, I. CREATION half -- seven tokens were created by Nest of Scarabs: C and D from the two activations that used an external/Bats sink, E and F from the single proliferate (two Nest triggers), and G, H, I from the three terminal activations. Six plus seven is THIRTEEN Bats triggers, each draining every opponent for 1 (119.3). Note what did NOT pay: Grizzly Bears' death and the three Insects killed by counters (D, F, H) are four deaths worth nothing to Bats -- but only THREE of those four are what a 'you control' death-metering drainer would have banked, because Grizzly Bears is an opponent's creature. Zulaport Cutthroat (stack 003) reads 'Whenever this creature or another creature you control dies' and Bastion of Remembrance (stack 002) reads 'Whenever a creature you control dies'; the Bears is not a creature you control, so its death does not match either ability's trigger event and neither ability triggers on it (603.2); the word 'you' on each of those objects refers to that object's controller -- you -- and not to opponent A (109.5). They bank the three counter-killed Insects D, F and H, which are tokens you control. The one drainer that would see all four is stack 004's Blood Artist, which is controller-agnostic -- 'Whenever this creature or another creature dies' carries no controller restriction, so the Bears' death does pay it (though it drains one 'target player', not each opponent). Final SBA check: every opponent is at 27, well above 0, so no player loses the game (704.5a); you are at 34 and drew only 6 cards from a 60-card library. Every Insect token that reached a graveyard, whether sacrificed or counter-killed, then ceased to exist (704.5d), which is why nine token deaths leave no trace in any graveyard.
    THIRTEEN Bats triggers. Each opponent loses 13 life: 40 -> 27. Total removed from the table: 13 x 4 = 52. Opposing life 160 -> 108 (32.5% of the table). THE COMPARISON, STATED PER SIBLING, BECAUSE THE TWO SIBLING BOARDS ARE NOT THE SAME BOARD. LIKE-FOR-LIKE AGAINST STACK 003: 003 is this exact three-body board with the drainer slot filled by Zulaport Cutthroat, and its checker-passed 7 drains / 28 opposing life is a WITH-proliferate figure, exactly as this line's is -- so this is 13 drains vs 7 and 52 pod life vs 28, +24 life, 86% more. STACK 002 IS A DIFFERENT BOARD AND ITS 28 IS NOT COMPARABLE TO THIS LINE: 002 carries a fourth body, the 1/1 white Human Soldier token that Bastion of Remembrance's own enters-the-battlefield ability creates (an enchantment cannot be a body), and its headline 7 drains / 28 life is its explicit NO-proliferate board invariant while this line spends the proliferate. 002's comparable with-proliferate figure is 8 drains / 32 opposing life, against which this is 13 drains vs 8 and 52 pod life vs 32, +20 life, +62.5%. Both comparisons favour Mirkwood Bats and neither is the other; 002, 003 and this stack are three boards and must never be presented as one result on one board. The margin's reason is structural, not incidental: a creation-and-sacrifice drainer is paid twice by a single Yawgmoth activation, whereas a death drainer is paid once per body, and Bats' 2/3 body adds two free counter sinks that a 1/1 drainer cannot offer. The price is your own life total: Bats gives you nothing back, so you finish at 34 rather than the 42 that 003's Zulaport Cutthroat -- which gains you 1 life per drain -- left you at (002 ends at 42 on its no-proliferate invariant and 43 on its proliferate line). NOT LETHAL, and not close.
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
    CR 704.5d — “If a token is in a zone other than the battlefield, it ceases to exist.”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
    CR 109.5 — “The words “you” and “your” on an object refer to the object’s controller”
After the line
Your life
34
Your battlefield
Yawgmoth, Thran Physician (2/4, no counters), Nest of Scarabs, Mirkwood Bats (0/1, two -1/-1 counters, alive), Swamp (tapped), Swamp (tapped), Swamp (untapped)
Opponents
Opponent: 27, Opponent: 27, Opponent: 27, Opponent: 27

Case A-009 Warren Soultrader + Gravecrawler + Bastion of Remembrance: is the drain unbounded, and what does one iteration cost?
Rules version — · status: cleared in ? review cycle(s) · tap to open Verified
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then resolve one full further iteration of the loop. (1) Can the loop be repeated an unbounded number of times from this board with no mana or life from outside it — specifically, does casting Gravecrawler from the graveyard have a legal permission each time, and does the Treasure created by each activation pay for that cast? (2) What is the exact net change per completed iteration to your life total, to each opponent's life total, and to the number of Treasures you control?
  1. Establish the stack order before anyone passes priority. Warren Soultrader's activated ability was put on the stack first (pos 0); its costs — pay 1 life, sacrifice another creature — were then paid, and Gravecrawler's death during that cost payment triggered Bastion of Remembrance. A triggered ability does nothing at the moment it triggers; it is put on the stack the next time a player would receive priority, i.e. after activation is complete, so it lands ON TOP of the ability that caused it (pos 1).
    Stack from bottom to top: [0] Warren Soultrader's ability (create a Treasure token), [1] Bastion of Remembrance's trigger (each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life). You are at 39 (the 1 life is already paid). Battlefield bodies: Warren Soultrader only — Gravecrawler is in your graveyard, not on the battlefield. Opponents: 40 / 40 / 40 / 40.
    CR 602.2a — “That ability is created on the stack as an object that’s not a card. It becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
    CR 117.2a — “Triggered abilities can trigger at any time, including while a spell is being cast, an ability is being activated, or a spell or ability is resolving.”
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority.”
    CR 405.2 — “Each time an object is put on the stack, it’s put on top of all objects already there.”
  2. Confirm the cost payment that produced this stack was legal, and locate Gravecrawler. Gravecrawler is a creature you control other than Warren Soultrader, so it satisfies 'another creature'; sacrificing it moved it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard. You had 40 life and the life component of the cost was 1, so that payment was legal.
    Gravecrawler is in your graveyard and no longer on the battlefield; your only body is Warren Soultrader. Your life is 39. (Whether the graveyard copy can be cast again is not decided here — that permission is established at step 6.)
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 119.4 — “If a cost or effect allows a player to pay an amount of life greater than 0, the player may do so only if their life total is greater than or equal to the amount of the payment.”
    CR 118.3b — “Paying life is done by subtracting the indicated amount of life from a player’s life total.”
  3. All players pass priority. The TOP object resolves first: Bastion of Remembrance's triggered ability.
    Each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life. Per opponent: 40 → 39, for all four seats. Across the pod that is 4 life removed in total (160 → 156 opposing life) — the per-seat figure (1) and the pod figure (4) are not interchangeable. You: 39 → 40.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  4. State-based actions are checked before any player receives priority again. No player is at 0 or less life (you 40; opponents 39 each), no creature has lethal damage, and nothing else on this board qualifies. No state-based action is performed and no ability has triggered, so the active player (you) receives priority with Warren Soultrader's ability still on the stack.
    No state-based action occurs. You hold priority; the Soultrader ability is the only object on the stack.
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
    CR 117.3b — “The active player receives priority after a spell or ability (other than a mana ability) resolves.”
  5. All players pass again. Warren Soultrader's activated ability resolves: create a Treasure token. Establish here, once, why nothing further triggers — this is what caps the drain at exactly 1 per iteration. A triggered ability triggers only when a game event or game state MATCHES its trigger event. Bastion of Remembrance has exactly two triggered abilities: 'When this enchantment enters, create a 1/1 white Human Soldier creature token' (its entry is long past) and 'Whenever a creature you control dies, each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life'. Bastion has no trigger event of the form 'a permanent enters' or 'a creature enters' at all, so creating a token — of any kind — matches neither ability. Creating the Treasure is therefore not a trigger event for Bastion, and the same rule governs steps 7 and 8 below.
    You create one Treasure token, an artifact token you control with "{T}, Sacrifice this token: Add one mana of any color." No ability triggers on its creation. Because the Treasure is an artifact and not a creature, its later sacrifice will not be a creature you control dying either, so it will not match Bastion's death trigger (applied at step 7). Treasures: 0 → 1. The stack is now empty. STACK AS GIVEN IS FULLY RESOLVED: you 40, each opponent 39, Treasures 1, Gravecrawler in graveyard, Warren Soultrader and Bastion of Remembrance on the battlefield.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 701.7a — “put the specified number of tokens with the specified characteristics onto the battlefield”
    CR 111.2 — “The player who creates a token is its owner. The token enters the battlefield under that player’s control.”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
  6. ITERATION 1, part (1) of the question — permission to cast Gravecrawler from the graveyard. Before you receive that priority the game checks state-based actions and performs none: with the stack empty the board is you at 40, each opponent at 39, Warren Soultrader and Bastion of Remembrance on the battlefield and the newly created Treasure on the battlefield as well, so no listed condition is met and no triggered ability is waiting to be put on the stack; the check therefore performs nothing and priority is handed back. You then receive priority with an empty stack in your precombat main phase; the phase does not end merely because the stack emptied. Gravecrawler's own static ability reads 'You may cast this card from your graveyard as long as you control a Zombie.' Two rules are needed, not one. 113.6f is the rule that makes an ability of this kind — one that modifies which zones that object can be cast from — function everywhere, including from the graveyard; without it, appealing to 604.6 alone would be circular, since the graveyard is a zone you could cast Gravecrawler from ONLY because of this very ability. 604.6 then describes the ordinary operation of such a static ability in that zone, and 601.3 is what the permission is needed for. The Zombie condition is met by Warren Soultrader, whose printed type line is Creature — Zombie Goblin Wizard; Soultrader is never sacrificed by this loop (its own ability requires sacrificing ANOTHER creature), so the permission is available on every iteration.
    Casting Gravecrawler from the graveyard is legal now and on every future iteration: a noninstant spell, your main phase, stack empty, priority held, permission granted by the card's own ability, which functions in the graveyard by 113.6f, with the Zombie requirement satisfied by the untouched Warren Soultrader.
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 601.3 — “A player can begin to cast a spell only if a rule or effect allows that player to cast it and no rule or effect prohibits that player from casting it.”
    CR 113.6f — “An object’s ability that restricts or modifies what zones that particular object can be played or cast from functions everywhere, even outside the game.”
    CR 604.6 — “Some static abilities apply while a card is in any zone that you could cast or play it from (usually your hand).”
    CR 117.1a — “A player may cast a noninstant spell during their main phase any time they have priority and the stack is empty.”
    CR 500.2 — “A phase or step in which players receive priority ends when the stack is empty and all players pass in succession.”
  7. ITERATION 1, part (1) continued — does the Treasure pay for the cast? Gravecrawler's mana cost is {B}. First establish that the Treasure's ability, '{T}, Sacrifice this token: Add one mana of any color', qualifies as a mana ability: it is an activated ability, it requires no target, it could add mana to your mana pool when it resolves, and it is not a loyalty ability — the three criteria of 605.1a, all met. Because it IS a mana ability, you may activate it while casting Gravecrawler, before costs are paid, and it does not use the stack. The Treasure is an artifact token, not a creature, so the summoning-sickness restriction — written only for a creature's activated ability with the tap symbol — does not reach it even though it was created this turn. Sacrificing it is not a creature you control dying, so no game event matches Bastion's trigger event (the rule stated at step 5) and Bastion does not trigger.
    {B} is added to your mana pool and spent on Gravecrawler's total cost. Treasures: 1 → 0. The sacrificed Treasure is a TOKEN now in a zone other than the battlefield, so it ceases to exist — this is the one state-based action this loop actually performs, and it happens at the next check, i.e. when a player would next receive priority once the cast is complete (the check stated at step 8). Nothing in part (2)'s ledger turns on that timing: the number of Treasures you control fell to 0 the moment the token left the battlefield. The same rule's parenthetical is why the trigger analysis above is safe — any applicable triggered ability would trigger BEFORE the token ceased to exist, and none is applicable, because Bastion's trigger event is a creature you control dying. No Bastion trigger. Gravecrawler is on the stack. Exactly one Treasure funds exactly one cast — the mana ledger of one iteration balances at zero.
    CR 605.1a — “An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn’t require a target (see rule 115.6), it could add mana to a player’s mana pool when it resolves, and it’s not a loyalty ability.”
    CR 601.2g — “the player then has a chance to activate mana abilities”
    CR 605.3b — “An activated mana ability doesn’t go on the stack, so it can’t be targeted, countered, or otherwise responded to. Rather, it resolves immediately after it is activated.”
    CR 302.6 — “A creature’s activated ability with the tap symbol or the untap symbol in its activation cost can’t be activated unless the creature has been under its controller’s control continuously since their most recent turn began.”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
    CR 106.4 — “When an effect instructs a player to add mana, that mana goes into a player’s mana pool. From there, it can be used to pay costs immediately”
    CR 601.2h — “The player pays the total cost.”
    CR 111.7 — “A token that’s in a zone other than the battlefield ceases to exist. This is a state-based action”
  8. All players pass. Gravecrawler, a permanent spell with no targets, resolves and enters the battlefield under your control. It is a new object with no relation to the card that was in the graveyard. Nothing dies during this cast or resolution, so no game event matches Bastion's 'Whenever a creature you control dies' trigger event, and Bastion does not trigger.
    Gravecrawler (2/1 Zombie) is on the battlefield. Bodies: Warren Soultrader + Gravecrawler = 2. Treasures 0. No Bastion trigger. Gravecrawler entering is a zone change, so before any player receives priority again the game checks state-based actions: nobody is at 0 or less life (you 40, each opponent 39), no creature has lethal damage, and no token is in any zone other than the battlefield — the Treasure ceased to exist at the previous check (step 7) and no token remains. Nothing applies and no ability is waiting, so the active player — you — receives priority.
    CR 608.3a — “If the object that’s resolving has no targets, it becomes a permanent and enters the battlefield under the control of the spell’s controller.”
    CR 400.7 — “An object that moves from one zone to another becomes a new object with no memory of, or relation to, its previous existence.”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 117.3b — “The active player receives priority after a spell or ability (other than a mana ability) resolves.”
  9. You receive priority and activate Warren Soultrader's ability again. Costs: pay 1 life (you have 40, so this is payable) and sacrifice another creature — Gravecrawler, which you control and which is not Soultrader itself. Summoning sickness is irrelevant: the sacrifice is a cost of Soultrader's ability, not of any ability of Gravecrawler with a tap symbol.
    You: 40 → 39. Gravecrawler moves from the battlefield to your graveyard — it dies, and it is a card, so it rests on top of that graveyard. Soultrader's ability is on the stack.
    CR 602.2a — “That ability is created on the stack as an object that’s not a card. It becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
    CR 119.4 — “If a cost or effect allows a player to pay an amount of life greater than 0, the player may do so only if their life total is greater than or equal to the amount of the payment.”
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 404.1 — “Any object that’s countered, discarded, destroyed, or sacrificed is put on top of its owner’s graveyard”
  10. Gravecrawler dying is a creature you control dying, so Bastion of Remembrance triggers. The trigger is put on the stack the next time a player would receive priority — after the activation is complete — so it goes above Soultrader's ability, exactly as in step 1.
    Stack: [0] Soultrader's ability, [1] Bastion's trigger. State-based actions are checked first and none apply (you 39, opponents 39 each).
    CR 700.4 — “The term dies means”
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority. See rule 117, “Timing and Priority.” The ability becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
  11. All players pass; Bastion's trigger resolves.
    Each opponent loses 1 life: 39 → 38, per seat, for all four. Pod total removed this iteration: 4 (156 → 152 opposing life). You gain 1: 39 → 40, which exactly refunds the 1 life paid in step 9.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  12. State-based actions are checked — nobody is at 0 or less — the active player receives priority, all players pass, and Warren Soultrader's ability resolves, creating a Treasure token.
    Treasures: 0 → 1. ONE FULL ITERATION IS COMPLETE. The board is identical to the end of step 5 in every respect except opponents' life: you 40, Warren Soultrader and Bastion of Remembrance on the battlefield, Gravecrawler in the graveyard, exactly 1 Treasure, stack empty, still your precombat main phase, still your priority.
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 117.3b — “The active player receives priority after a spell or ability (other than a mana ability) resolves.”
    CR 701.7a — “put the specified number of tokens with the specified characteristics onto the battlefield”
  13. ANSWER TO PART (2) — the net ledger of one completed iteration, read off steps 6–12. Life paid: 1 (step 9). Life gained: 1 (step 11). Treasures created: 1 (step 12). Treasures sacrificed: 1 (step 7). Drain: 1 per opponent (step 11), and exactly one drain per iteration because the only event matching Bastion's trigger is Gravecrawler's death (steps 5, 7, 8, 10).
    Per completed iteration — YOUR life total: net 0 (transient dip to 39 between step 9 and step 11; you never fall below 39 and never approach 0). EACH OPPONENT'S life total: −1. TREASURES you control: net 0 (steady state exactly 1 at the top of each iteration). Pod-wide, one iteration removes 4 life across the four seats; that pod figure must never be quoted as a per-seat figure.
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
    CR 118.3b — “Paying life is done by subtracting the indicated amount of life from a player’s life total.”
    CR 701.7a — “put the specified number of tokens with the specified characteristics onto the battlefield”
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
  14. ANSWER TO PART (1) — is the loop unbounded from this board with no outside mana or life? Every resource an iteration consumes it also replaces, in the same iteration, from inside the loop: the {B} for Gravecrawler comes from the Treasure the previous activation made, whose ability is a mana ability (steps 5, 7, 12); the 1 life for Soultrader comes back from Bastion (steps 9, 11); Gravecrawler is a card, so it returns to the graveyard each time and its own ability functions there (steps 6, 9); the Zombie that grants the graveyard permission is Warren Soultrader, which the loop never sacrifices (step 6). Nothing in the loop draws on the three tapped Swamps, the empty hand, or the library. The loop is therefore repeatable an unbounded number of times, and because it can be repeated indefinitely the shortcut rules let you declare a number of iterations rather than perform them.
    YES on both halves of part (1): the graveyard cast has a legal permission on every iteration, and each activation's Treasure exactly funds the next cast. The loop is self-funding and unbounded in the rules sense; in practice it terminates because the drain is not — the operative threshold is 0 life, so 40 drain events bring a 40-life opponent to exactly 0, and one drain has already resolved on the stack as given, leaving 39 further iterations after step 5 to put all four opponents at 0 simultaneously, whereupon each of them loses the game. 'Infinite lifeloss' as a containment tag is CONFIRMED for this board, with the arithmetic that matters: 1 life per opponent per iteration, 4 across the pod per iteration, 40 drain events to kill each seat.
    CR 732.1b — “the shortcut rules can be used to determine how many times those actions are repeated without having to actually perform them, and how the loop is broken”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
After the line
Your battlefield
Warren Soultrader (3/3), Bastion of Remembrance, Treasure token (1), Swamp (tapped), Swamp (tapped), Swamp (tapped)
