Case A-010 Mikaeus + Yawgmoth: does the -1/-1 counter annihilate undying's +1/+1 and re-arm the body, and what bounds the loop?
Rules version — · status: cleared in ? review cycle(s) · tap to open Verified
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack. When Yawgmoth's ability puts a -1/-1 counter on Gravecrawler, which already carries a +1/+1 counter, what does the next state-based-action check do to those counters — and is Gravecrawler afterwards eligible for undying the next time it dies? Then state what bounds repetition of this loop.
  1. No player responds; priority passes and the stack resolves top-down (last in, first out). The topmost object is pos 2, the undying triggered ability granted to Viscera Seer by Mikaeus. Below it sit pos 1 (Bastion of Remembrance's death trigger) and pos 0 (Yawgmoth's activated ability, both of whose costs — 1 life, sacrifice Viscera Seer — are already paid).
    Resolution order is: undying trigger, then Bastion trigger, then Yawgmoth's ability. Viscera Seer is in the graveyard and is NOT a body on the battlefield; the board entering this resolution is three creatures — Yawgmoth 2/4, Mikaeus 5/5, Gravecrawler (2/1 printed, +1/+1 from Mikaeus, one +1/+1 counter = 4/3).
    CR 608.1 — “Each time all players pass in succession, the spell or ability on top of the stack resolves.”
    CR 117.4 — “the spell or ability on top of the stack resolves”
  2. The undying trigger resolves. Undying is a leaves-the-battlefield ability with an intervening 'if' clause: 'if it had no +1/+1 counters on it'. Viscera Seer is now a new object in the graveyard, so the condition is evaluated against the creature as it last existed ON THE BATTLEFIELD — where it had no counters at all (it was 1/1 printed, 2/2 only because of Mikaeus's static +1/+1, which is not a counter).
    The condition is true both at trigger time and again on resolution, so the ability resolves: Viscera Seer returns to the battlefield under its owner's control with a +1/+1 counter on it. It returns as a new object with no memory of its previous existence.
    CR 702.93a — “if it had no +1/+1 counters on it, return it to the battlefield under its owner’s control with a +1/+1 counter on it”
    CR 603.4 — “If the ability triggers, it checks the stated condition again as it resolves. If the condition isn’t true at that time, the ability is removed from the stack and does nothing.”
    CR 603.10a — “Some zone-change triggers look back in time. These are leaves-the-battlefield abilities”
    CR 608.2h — “if it’s no longer in that zone, or if the effect has moved it from a public zone to a hidden zone, the effect uses the object’s last known information”
    CR 400.7 — “An object that moves from one zone to another becomes a new object with no memory of, or relation to, its previous existence.”
  3. Before any player receives priority again, state-based actions are checked. The returned Viscera Seer is 1/1 printed with one +1/+1 counter and Mikaeus's +1/+1 static bonus, both applied in layer 7c, so it is 3/3. Its toughness is above 0 and no other SBA condition applies.
    No state-based action is performed. The board is now four bodies: Yawgmoth 2/4, Mikaeus 5/5, Gravecrawler 4/3 (one +1/+1 counter), Viscera Seer 3/3 (one +1/+1 counter). Because Viscera Seer now HAS a +1/+1 counter, it is not eligible for undying if it dies again in this state.
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event”
    CR 613.4c — “Layer 7c: Effects and counters that modify power and/or toughness (but don’t set power and/or toughness to a specific number or value) are applied.”
  4. Bastion of Remembrance's trigger resolves: 'each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life.' It triggered on Viscera Seer's death during cost payment.
    PER SEAT: each of the four opponents loses 1 life, 40 -> 39. POD TOTAL: 4 life removed across the pod, 160 -> 156. These are not interchangeable figures. You gain 1 life: 39 -> 40. No opponent is at 0 or less, so no player loses the game on the following state-based-action check.
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
  5. Yawgmoth's activated ability resolves. Its target, Gravecrawler, never left the battlefield and its characteristics have not changed in any way that matters to 'up to one target creature', so the target is still legal. The ability puts a -1/-1 counter on Gravecrawler and you draw a card.
    Gravecrawler now carries one +1/+1 counter AND one -1/-1 counter. Its power/toughness at this instant: 2/1 printed, +1/+1 from Mikaeus and the two counters all applied in layer 7c, netting 3/2. You draw one card: hand 0 -> 1, library 60 -> 59.
    CR 608.2b — “If the spell or ability specifies targets, it checks whether the targets are still legal.”
    CR 121.1 — “A player draws a card by putting the top card of their library into their hand.”
    CR 613.4c — “Effects and counters that modify power and/or toughness”
  6. THE CRUX. The stack is now empty and you would receive priority, so state-based actions are checked first. Gravecrawler has both a +1/+1 counter and a -1/-1 counter on it.
    N = 1 (the smaller of the two counts), so one +1/+1 counter and one -1/-1 counter are removed from Gravecrawler simultaneously as a state-based action. Gravecrawler ends the check with ZERO counters of either kind, at 3/2 (2/1 printed plus Mikaeus's static +1/+1). The 0-or-less-toughness SBA does NOT apply: its toughness is 2. The -1/-1 counter therefore did not shrink Gravecrawler at all in net terms — it annihilated the +1/+1 counter and both were removed.
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 704.5q — “If a permanent has both a +1/+1 counter and a -1/-1 counter on it, N +1/+1 and N -1/-1 counters are removed from it, where N is the smaller of the number of +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters on it.”
    CR 122.3 — “as a state-based action, where N is the smaller of the number of +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters on it”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  7. Undying eligibility for Gravecrawler is re-armed. Gravecrawler is a Zombie (non-Human), so Mikaeus continues to grant it undying. Undying's intervening 'if' clause asks whether it had no +1/+1 counters on it, evaluated against the creature as it last existed on the battlefield.
    YES — Gravecrawler is eligible for undying the next time it dies. It now has no +1/+1 counters, so if it is sacrificed in this state the condition is true both when the trigger would go on the stack and again on resolution, and it returns with a fresh +1/+1 counter. Symmetrically, Viscera Seer is currently NOT eligible: it carries the +1/+1 counter from step 2 until something removes it. Exactly one of the two non-Human bodies is armed at any time, and Yawgmoth's -1/-1 counter is what flips which one.
    CR 702.93a — “if it had no +1/+1 counters on it, return it to the battlefield under its owner’s control with a +1/+1 counter on it”
    CR 603.4 — “it checks the stated condition again as it resolves”
    CR 608.2h — “the effect uses the object’s last known information”
  8. THE SWAP, VERIFIED ON THE OTHER BODY. Each further iteration is one activation of Yawgmoth: pay 1 life, sacrifice the body that currently carries NO +1/+1 counter (it returns via undying with one), and put the -1/-1 counter on the OTHER body. On the very first swap that other body is VISCERA SEER, which is printed 1/1 — smaller than Gravecrawler — so the step-6 state-based-action check must be re-performed for it rather than assumed. At that check Viscera Seer is 1/1 printed, +1/+1 from Mikaeus, one +1/+1 counter and one -1/-1 counter, all in layer 7c: power 1+1+1-1 = 2, toughness 1+1+1-1 = 2.
    Toughness is 2, not 0 or less, so the creature-death state-based action does NOT apply to Viscera Seer and it survives the check. In that same single state-based-action event established in step 6, N = 1 counter of each kind is removed, leaving Viscera Seer a 2/2 with ZERO counters — re-armed for undying exactly as Gravecrawler was in step 6, while Gravecrawler now carries the +1/+1 counter from its own undying return. The alternation therefore holds in both directions: the pair swaps roles every activation, and neither body ever dies to the counter itself.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard. Regeneration can’t replace this event.”
    CR 704.5q — “N +1/+1 and N -1/-1 counters are removed from it, where N is the smaller of the number of +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters on it”
    CR 613.4c — “Effects and counters that modify power and/or toughness”
  9. WHAT BOUNDS REPETITION, AND THE ONE TRAP ON THIS BOARD. First, that the line repeats at all: after step 6's state-based-action check the stack is empty, it is your precombat main phase and priority returns to you, and a player may activate an activated ability any time they have priority. Yawgmoth's ability carries no mana symbol in its cost and no activation restriction, so it may be activated again at {0} available — that is what makes steps 8-10 repetition claims rather than a one-shot. Three quantities then bound the repetition, and the one further free sacrifice outlet on this board is a TRAP, not a bonus.
    (1) LIFE is not the bound: you pay 1 life per activation and Bastion returns exactly 1 life per creature death, so your total is flat at 40 and the 119.4 requirement that you have at least the life you are paying is never threatened. (2) DRAIN is the clock: one creature dies per activation, so each activation removes 1 life PER OPPONENT and 4 across the pod. From the post-resolution 39 per seat, 39 further activations put every opponent at 0 and they lose to state-based actions; that is 156 pod life removed. (3) The LIBRARY is the hard bound and it is finite: Yawgmoth's ability reads 'Put a -1/-1 counter on up to one target creature and draw a card' with no 'may', and its controller follows those instructions in the order written, so the draw is MANDATORY and cannot be declined. With 59 cards left you may activate at most 59 more times; a 60th activation would attempt a draw from an empty library and YOU lose the game at the next state-based-action check. The loop is therefore NOT infinite — a 59-activation engine that needs 39, with 20 activations of margin. (4) THE SECOND OUTLET IS A TRAP — CORRECTED FROM THE PREVIOUS PASS, WHICH CALLED IT 'STRICTLY FAVOURABLE'. Viscera Seer is back on the battlefield from step 2 and has its own activated ability, 'Sacrifice a creature: Scry 1' — an activated ability written cost-then-effect whose cost contains no mana symbol, so it is free even at {0} available, and each use kills a creature and so triggers Bastion for another 1 per seat while consuming no library. READ THE COST EXACTLY, BECAUSE THE PREVIOUS PASS DID NOT: the Seer's cost is 'Sacrifice A creature', NOT 'another creature' as Yawgmoth's cost is. The Seer may therefore eat ANY creature you control — including Mikaeus, including Yawgmoth, and including ITSELF. That distinction is what makes the finisher count 5 rather than 3. It is nevertheless ENGINE-ENDING if used mid-loop, and a pilot who reads 'free extra drain' and fires it kills their own combo. WHY: the engine's steady state is exactly one ARMED body (no +1/+1 counter, undying-eligible) and one LOADED body (one +1/+1 counter, not eligible), and only Yawgmoth's -1/-1 counter can strip a +1/+1 counter — it does so via the step-6 state-based action, one counter per activation, which is why one Yawgmoth activation re-arms exactly one body. The Seer's ability places no counter at all. So if you sacrifice the ARMED body to the Seer, undying returns it WITH a +1/+1 counter and now BOTH bodies are loaded and NEITHER is undying-eligible; and if you sacrifice the LOADED body to the Seer, its undying trigger's intervening 'if' is false and that body is gone PERMANENTLY. THE TWO BRANCHES DIFFER IN WHERE THE PERMANENT LOSS FALLS, AND THE PREVIOUS PASS'S 'either way the next Yawgmoth activation must sacrifice a counter-bearing body' IS FALSE IN THE SECOND: in branch A (armed body eaten) nothing is lost yet, both bodies carry counters, and the NEXT Yawgmoth activation must sacrifice a counter-bearing body, which dies for good; in branch B (loaded body eaten) the permanent loss has ALREADY happened and the survivor is ARMED, so the next Yawgmoth sacrifice loses nothing permanently — it returns loaded. THE CONCLUSION IS UNCHANGED, because both branches converge on the same state, ONE alternation body, and one body can never sustain the alternation. An activation kills exactly one body and yields exactly one -1/-1 counter, which strips exactly one +1/+1 counter, and it can never strip the counter on the body it just ate: that body was sacrificed to pay the cost, so if you had targeted it, on resolution it is no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted and is an illegal target; and if you targeted anything else, its +1/+1 counter simply stays. Re-arming therefore always costs a DIFFERENT body's death, and with one alternation body left the only other creatures are Mikaeus — whose death removes undying from that body entirely, since the grant is his — and Yawgmoth, which its own 'another creature' cost may not sacrifice. The alternation is over within one or two further activations in both branches. ONE mid-loop Seer sacrifice therefore buys 1 drain per seat and costs you roughly 34 of the 39 activations you still need: you collect about five drains from the collapsing board instead of the 39 the intact alternation would have delivered. THE ONLY SAFE USE IS AS A FINISHER, after the alternation is no longer needed — and at that point at most FIVE free Seer sacrifices exist in total, CORRECTED FROM THREE, which omitted Mikaeus and Yawgmoth. The finisher board is exactly four bodies: Yawgmoth, Mikaeus, and the two alternation bodies, one armed and one loaded, one of which is the Seer itself. Order it so the SEER EATS ITSELF LAST, since its outlet leaves the battlefield with it: (1) sacrifice the ARMED body — it dies for a Bastion drain and undying returns it LOADED, no permanent loss; (2)-(4) sacrifice, in any order, the three bodies other than the Seer that are now on the battlefield — the loaded alternation body (its intervening 'if' is false, permanent), Mikaeus (his own grant reads 'OTHER non-Human creatures you control get +1/+1 and have undying', so it excludes himself and he has no undying, permanent) and Yawgmoth (a Legendary Creature — Human Cleric per cards.json, so Mikaeus's non-Human grant never reached it, permanent); (5) sacrifice the Seer to itself, permanent. Five deaths, five Bastion drains, zero library consumed. The count is the same 5 in EITHER orientation of which alternation body is armed: if the Seer is the armed body, it is step (1) and it comes straight back to keep working. SIX IS IMPOSSIBLE: four bodies plus exactly ONE undying return, because only one body is armed at a time and neither Yawgmoth (Human) nor Mikaeus (excluded by his own 'Other') can undy. ACCOUNTING CONSEQUENCE: margin = 59 - (39 - k) = 20 + k, where k is the number of finisher Seer sacrifices taken and 0 <= k <= 5. The margin is 20 activations clean and at most 25 with all five finisher sacrifices — a bounded range, NOT an open-ended lower bound as an earlier pass asserted, and NOT the 20-23 the previous pass computed from a count of three. Nothing else on this board caps the loop: the loop costs no mana, Gravecrawler cannot be recast from the graveyard because that requires {B} and mana available is {0}, and Mikaeus need only remain on the battlefield, which it does because Yawgmoth's cost sacrifices 'another creature' and you always choose one of the two designated bodies.
    CR 117.1b — “A player may activate an activated ability any time they have priority.”
    CR 119.4 — “If a player pays life, the payment is subtracted from their life total; in other words, the player loses that much life.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 704.5b — “If a player attempted to draw a card from a library with no cards in it since the last time state-based actions were checked, that player loses the game.”
    CR 602.1 — “Activated abilities have a cost and an effect. They are written as “[Cost]: [Effect.] [Activation instructions (if any).]””
    CR 702.93a — “if it had no +1/+1 counters on it, return it to the battlefield under its owner’s control with a +1/+1 counter on it”
    CR 704.5q — “N +1/+1 and N -1/-1 counters are removed from it, where N is the smaller of the number of +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters on it”
    CR 608.2b — “A target that’s no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted is illegal.”
  10. HOW THE KILL BECOMES A WIN. The 39th further activation brings every opponent from 1 to 0 life; the drain is one simultaneous event per Bastion resolution, so all four seats reach 0 together and all four lose the game at the next state-based-action check. You are still in the game at that moment: your life is flat at 40 (step 9, item 1), and you have used 39 of the 59 draws the library permits, so the deck-out state-based action has not been met.
    With all four of your opponents having left the game and you still in it, you win the game immediately. This is the rule that converts four simultaneous losses in a five-player pod into a win; the loss of the opponents alone does not state it. The Mikaeus + Yawgmoth line is a verified kill on this board.
    CR 104.2a — “A player still in the game wins the game if that player’s opponents have all left the game. This happens immediately and overrides all effects that would preclude that player from winning the game.”
After the line
Your life
40
Your battlefield
Yawgmoth, Thran Physician (2/4, commander), Mikaeus, the Unhallowed (5/5), Gravecrawler (3/2 — 2/1 printed +1/+1 from Mikaeus; NO counters; undying re-armed — this is the ARMED body), Viscera Seer (3/3 — 1/1 printed, one +1/+1 counter, +1/+1 from Mikaeus; undying NOT available while that counter remains — this is the LOADED body; its own free sacrifice outlet is live but is a TRAP mid-loop, see step 9 item 4; at most FIVE free Seer sacrifices exist as a finisher), Bastion of Remembrance, Swamp (tapped) x4
Opponents
Opponent 1: 39, Opponent 2: 39, Opponent 3: 39, Opponent 4: 39

Case A-011 Gravecrawler + Phyrexian Altar + Carrion Feeder + Bastion of Remembrance: is the drain unbounded and does it cost anything?
Rules version — · status: cleared in ? review cycle(s) · tap to open Verified
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then resolve one full further iteration. Can the loop be repeated an unbounded number of times from this board with no mana and no life from outside it, and what is the exact net change per completed iteration to your life total and to each opponent's life total?
  1. Establish what is on the stack, and check it against the corrected scenario. Phyrexian Altar's ability — 'Sacrifice a creature: Add one mana of any color.' — requires no target, could add mana when it resolves, and is not a loyalty ability, so it is a mana ability. A mana ability never uses the stack: it resolved immediately when it was activated and put {B} into your mana pool, where it stays as unspent mana until the end of the step or phase. Bastion of Remembrance's ability triggered on Gravecrawler's death, and that death happened DURING the payment of the Altar's activation cost — i.e. while an ability was being activated, which is expressly a time at which an ability may trigger. Nothing happened at that moment; the trigger was held and put on the stack the next time a player would receive priority.
    The scenario now matches the rules and scenario-facts, so there is nothing to report as a disagreement: the stack holds exactly ONE object — Bastion of Remembrance's triggered ability at pos 0 — and {B} of your chosen colour (black, for Gravecrawler) is already in your mana pool with both Swamps tapped. Board entering the resolution: ONE creature body, Carrion Feeder; Phyrexian Altar and Bastion of Remembrance are the other permanents; Gravecrawler is listed only as the permanent already sacrificed to pay the cost and is in the graveyard, not on the battlefield. Four opponents at 40 each.
    CR 605.1a — “An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn’t require a target”
    CR 605.3b — “An activated mana ability doesn’t go on the stack, so it can’t be targeted, countered, or otherwise responded to. Rather, it resolves immediately after it is activated.”
    CR 106.4 — “When an effect instructs a player to add mana, that mana goes into a player’s mana pool. From there, it can be used to pay costs immediately, or it can stay in the player’s mana pool as unspent mana. Each player’s mana pool empties at the end of each step and phase”
    CR 117.2a — “Triggered abilities can trigger at any time, including while a spell is being cast, an ability is being activated, or a spell or ability is resolving. (See rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities.”) However, nothing actually happens at the time an ability triggers. Each time a player would receive priority, each ability that has triggered but hasn’t yet been put on the stack is put on the stack.”
  2. Confirm the cost that produced this stack and locate every permanent. Phyrexian Altar's only activation cost is 'Sacrifice a creature'; Gravecrawler was that creature, so it moved from the battlefield to your graveyard and is NOT on the battlefield. No life was paid — the Altar's cost contains no life component. The bodies remaining on your battlefield number ONE: Carrion Feeder (1/1, no counters), which is never the sacrifice.
    Battlefield: Carrion Feeder (Zombie 1/1), Phyrexian Altar, Bastion of Remembrance, two tapped Swamps. Graveyard: Gravecrawler. Hand: empty. Your life is still 40 — unlike a Warren Soultrader activation, nothing here charges life. Gravecrawler's move from battlefield to graveyard is the game event 'dies', which is what Bastion's trigger condition matched.
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 700.4 — “The term dies means”
  3. All players pass priority. The top (and only) object on the stack resolves: Bastion of Remembrance's triggered ability — 'each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life.' Four opponents each lose 1; you gain 1.
    Each opponent 40 -> 39 (per seat -1; across the four-seat pod -4, 160 -> 156). You 40 -> 41. The stack is now empty and {B} is still unspent in your mana pool.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  4. State-based actions are checked before any player receives priority again. No player is at 0 or less life (you 41, each opponent 39). Carrion Feeder is a 1/1 with no counters and no damage. No other listed condition applies. The active player — you — then receives priority with an empty stack, in your precombat main phase.
    No state-based action is performed, no player loses the game, nothing is put on the stack. You hold priority at sorcery speed. Note that no step or phase has ended, so the {B} from step 1 has not been lost.
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
    CR 117.3b — “The active player receives priority after a spell or ability (other than a mana ability) resolves.”
  5. ONE FULL FURTHER ITERATION, part 1 — permission to cast Gravecrawler from the graveyard. Casting requires a rule or effect to allow it. Gravecrawler's own ability, 'You may cast this card from your graveyard as long as you control a Zombie,' is an ability that modifies what zone the card can be cast from, so it functions in the graveyard, and it is one of the limited static abilities that apply outside the hand. Its condition is checked now: you control Carrion Feeder, whose type line is Creature — Zombie. Timing is satisfied: Gravecrawler is a creature (noninstant) spell, it is your precombat main phase, you have priority and the stack is empty.
    Permission granted on this iteration and on every future one, because the Zombie condition is met by Carrion Feeder, which this loop never sacrifices. This is the same machinery verified on stack 009, where the Zombie was Warren Soultrader; here the qualifying Zombie is Carrion Feeder and it costs nothing to maintain.
    CR 601.3 — “A player can begin to cast a spell only if a rule or effect allows that player to cast it and no rule or effect prohibits that player from casting it.”
    CR 113.6f — “An object’s ability that restricts or modifies what zones that particular object can be played or cast from functions everywhere, even outside the game.”
    CR 604.6 — “Some static abilities apply while a card is in any zone that you could cast or play it from (usually your hand).”
    CR 117.1a — “A player may cast a noninstant spell during their main phase any time they have priority and the stack is empty.”
  6. ONE FULL FURTHER ITERATION, part 2 — paying for the cast. Gravecrawler's mana cost is {B}. The total cost is {B} with no additional or alternative costs. You pay it with the {B} already in your mana pool — the mana the Altar made from sacrificing the previous Gravecrawler. No mana ability needs to be activated during the casting process, and no land needs to untap: both Swamps stay tapped and you never draw mana from outside this board.
    Mana pool: {B} -> empty. Gravecrawler is on the stack as a creature spell. THIS is why the line is self-funding: the sacrifice that kills Gravecrawler is the same act that pays to bring it back.
    CR 601.2h — “The player pays the total cost.”
    CR 106.4 — “it can be used to pay costs immediately, or it can stay in the player’s mana pool as unspent mana”
  7. All players pass. Gravecrawler, a permanent spell with no targets, resolves and enters the battlefield under your control. It is a new object with no memory of having been in the graveyard. Check every trigger condition on the board against this event: cards.json gives Bastion of Remembrance TWO triggered abilities — 'When this enchantment enters, create a 1/1 white Human Soldier creature token.' and 'Whenever a creature you control dies, each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life.' An ability triggers only when a game event matches ITS trigger event: the first names Bastion's own entry, and Bastion is already on the battlefield and does not re-enter, while the second names a death, not an entry. State-based actions are checked and the active player receives priority.
    Battlefield: Carrion Feeder, Gravecrawler (2/1), Phyrexian Altar, Bastion of Remembrance, two tapped Swamps. Graveyard: empty. Bodies: TWO. Nothing triggers on Gravecrawler entering — NOT because Bastion has only one trigger condition (it has two), but because neither of its two conditions matches a creature other than Bastion entering. The board as given, and scenario-facts, list Carrion Feeder as the only creature body, so no Human Soldier token from Bastion's enters trigger is present on this board; that trigger is spent regardless, since Bastion is not entering. Life totals unchanged: you 41, each opponent 39.
    CR 608.3a — “If the object that’s resolving has no targets, it becomes a permanent and enters the battlefield under the control of the spell’s controller.”
    CR 400.7 — “An object that moves from one zone to another becomes a new object with no memory of, or relation to, its previous existence.”
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
  8. ONE FULL FURTHER ITERATION, part 3 — activate Phyrexian Altar again with priority, paying its cost by sacrificing Gravecrawler (not Carrion Feeder). The ability is a mana ability, so it does not go on the stack and resolves immediately; you choose black.
    Gravecrawler moves from the battlefield to your graveyard. {B} is added to your mana pool, funding the NEXT cast. Carrion Feeder is untouched and gains no counter — it is not the sacrifice outlet in this line, only the Zombie that grants permission. No opponent ever gets a window to respond to the mana ability itself.
    CR 605.3a — “A player may activate an activated mana ability whenever they have priority”
    CR 605.3b — “An activated mana ability doesn’t go on the stack, so it can’t be targeted, countered, or otherwise responded to. Rather, it resolves immediately after it is activated.”
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
  9. Gravecrawler being put into the graveyard from the battlefield is a creature you control dying, which matches Bastion of Remembrance's trigger event, so the ability triggers. As in step 1, this death occurs while an ability is being activated — it is the payment of Phyrexian Altar's cost — and a triggered ability may trigger at such a time; nothing happens at that moment. The mana ability's resolution gives no player priority, so the trigger waits. You activated the mana ability while you had priority, so you receive priority again immediately afterward, and that is the next time a player would receive priority: the trigger goes on the stack then.
    Bastion's triggered ability is the only object on the stack. Exactly ONE trigger occurs per iteration: of Bastion's two triggered abilities only the death trigger can match here (its enters trigger needs Bastion itself to enter, and Bastion has not moved), Carrion Feeder's counter ability is activated rather than triggered and is never used in this line, so nothing else in the loop adds drain.
    CR 700.4 — “The term dies means”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
    CR 117.2a — “Triggered abilities can trigger at any time, including while a spell is being cast, an ability is being activated, or a spell or ability is resolving. (See rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities.”) However, nothing actually happens at the time an ability triggers. Each time a player would receive priority, each ability that has triggered but hasn’t yet been put on the stack is put on the stack.”
    CR 117.3c — “If a player has priority when they cast a spell, activate an ability, or take a special action, that player receives priority afterward.”
  10. All players pass; Bastion's trigger resolves. Each opponent loses 1 life, you gain 1 life. State-based actions are then checked before priority is passed again.
    Each opponent 39 -> 38 (per seat -1; pod 156 -> 152). You 41 -> 42. No player is at 0 or less, so no player loses the game yet. The board is now EXACTLY the state at step 4: Carrion Feeder, Phyrexian Altar, Bastion of Remembrance, two tapped Swamps, Gravecrawler in the graveyard, {B} in the pool, stack empty, you holding sorcery-speed priority. One full iteration is complete.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
  11. ANSWER — is the loop unbounded, and what does one iteration cost? Read the ledger off steps 5-10. Mana: {B} spent on the cast, {B} produced by the sacrifice — net 0, and no land ever untaps. Life paid: none (Phyrexian Altar's cost is a sacrifice only). Cards: none drawn, so the 60-card library is never touched. Bodies: Gravecrawler leaves the graveyard and returns to it; Carrion Feeder never moves and never gains a counter. Nothing in the iteration is consumed, so the set of actions can be repeated indefinitely and the shortcut rules apply.
    YES, unbounded with no mana and no life from outside the board. Exact net change per completed iteration: YOUR life +1; EACH opponent -1 per seat, which is -4 across the four-seat pod — the per-seat and pod figures are not interchangeable and neither may be quoted as the other. Kill maths, per seat: each opponent starts at 40 and needs 40 drain events. The trigger already on the stack was event 1 (step 3), so 39 further iterations reduce all four opponents to 0 simultaneously; the state-based-action check after that 40th trigger resolves makes all four lose the game at once. You finish at 40 + 40 = 80 life. The Commander Spellbook line (popularity 60,592) is CONFIRMED as a true unbounded drain for this exact board.
    CR 732.1b — “the shortcut rules can be used to determine how many times those actions are repeated without having to actually perform them, and how the loop is broken.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
After the line
Your battlefield
Carrion Feeder (1/1, no counters), Phyrexian Altar, Bastion of Remembrance, Swamp (tapped), Swamp (tapped)

Case A-012 Exquisite Blood + South Wind Avatar: the loop is mandatory — does it kill the pod or draw the game?
Rules version — · status: cleared in ? review cycle(s) · tap to open Verified
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack. Once South Wind Avatar's lifegain trigger resolves, Exquisite Blood and South Wind Avatar's second ability form a mandatory loop. Does that loop continue forever and draw the game, or does something end it — and if it ends, what exactly ends it and at what life totals?
  1. Starting position. Carrion Feeder's activated ability is on the stack at pos 0 (bottom). Activation put the ability on the stack FIRST, as the topmost object at that moment; the activation cost (sacrifice a creature) was then paid as part of the same activation process, which is why Insect token X is NOT on the battlefield. Your bodies are South Wind Avatar and Carrion Feeder (2 creatures). Exquisite Blood is your only other permanent. Opponents: 4 seats at 40 life each (per-seat 40; pod total 160). You are at 40.
    The ability is on the stack and the Insect has already been sacrificed. No player received priority at any point during the activation process, so nothing has resolved and no state-based action has yet been checked.
    CR 602.2a — “It becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
    CR 602.2b — “The remainder of the process for activating an ability is identical to the process for casting a spell listed in rules 601.2b–i”
    CR 601.2h — “The player pays the total cost.”
  2. Insect token X died on the battlefield during cost payment, so South Wind Avatar's first ability ('Whenever another creature you control dies, you gain life equal to its toughness') triggered at that moment. The next time a player would receive priority, the game FIRST performs state-based actions and only THEN puts waiting triggered abilities on the stack (117.5) -- that ordering is what makes the following two things happen in this order. State-based action: the token is now in the graveyard, a zone other than the battlefield, so it ceases to exist. Then the already-triggered death trigger is put on the stack above the Carrion Feeder ability, so it is at pos 1 (top) and resolves first.
    Insect token X ceases to exist (it is not in the graveyard thereafter). Stack top-down: [South Wind Avatar death trigger] over [Carrion Feeder activated ability]. The trigger is unaffected: it triggered on the death event, which happened while the token was on the battlefield. Because the token is gone before the trigger resolves, its toughness must come from last known information -- see step 3.
    CR 603.2 — “that ability automatically triggers”
    CR 704.5d — “If a token is in a zone other than the battlefield, it ceases to exist.”
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack”
    CR 603.3 — “the next time a player would receive priority”
    CR 603.3 — “The ability becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
  3. Every opponent passes priority (scenario assumption: 'No player responds to anything; every opponent passes priority throughout'). All players having passed in succession, the top object resolves: the death trigger. It requires information from a specific object -- the dead Insect token -- which is no longer in the zone it was expected to be in (it ceased to exist at step 2), so the effect uses that object's LAST KNOWN INFORMATION: toughness 1. You therefore gain 1 life. This same pass-and-resolve mechanism drives every one of the roughly eighty resolutions in steps 5 through 10 below; it is not restated at each one.
    You: 40 -> 41, on the token's last known toughness of 1. Opponents unchanged (40 each; pod 160).
    CR 117.4 — “If all players pass in succession (that is, if all players pass without taking any actions in between passing), the spell or ability on top of the stack resolves”
    CR 608.2h — “if it’s in the public zone it was expected to be in; if it’s no longer in that zone, or if the effect has moved it from a public zone to a hidden zone, the effect uses the object’s last known information.”
    CR 608.2c — “follows its instructions in the order written”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life”
  4. That life gain is a life-gain event for you, so South Wind Avatar's SECOND ability ('Whenever you gain life, each opponent loses 1 life') triggers once. It is put on the stack above the Carrion Feeder ability. Note the direction: this ability watches YOU gaining life, not opponents losing it.
    Stack top-down: [South Wind Avatar drain trigger #1] over [Carrion Feeder activated ability].
    CR 603.2 — “that ability automatically triggers”
    CR 117.5 — “Then triggered abilities are put on the stack”
  5. All players pass again (step 3) and drain #1 resolves: each opponent loses 1 life. This is one effect hitting all four seats at once.
    Per seat: 40 -> 39. Pod total: 160 -> 156 (4 life removed across the pod, 1 per seat). You are still at 41.
    CR 608.2c — “follows its instructions in the order written”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life”
  6. Exquisite Blood ('Whenever an opponent loses life, you gain that much life') sees four opponents losing life. That one happening contains four occurrences of its trigger event -- one per opponent -- so it triggers four times, each for 1 life. Note the direction: Exquisite Blood watches an OPPONENT losing life, not you gaining it. Because these four have triggered simultaneously, they are placed on the stack by their controller, in APNAP order, in any order that player chooses; you control all four and they are identical (each gains you 1 life), so the ordering choice changes nothing.
    Four Exquisite Blood triggers are put on the stack above the Carrion Feeder ability, in an order you choose that is immaterial. Stack top-down: [EB x4] over [Carrion Feeder ability].
    CR 603.2c — “An ability triggers only once each time its trigger event occurs. However, it can trigger repeatedly if one event contains multiple occurrences.”
    CR 700.1 — “Anything that happens in a game is an event. Multiple events may take place during the resolution of a spell or ability.”
    CR 603.3b — “each player, in APNAP order, puts each triggered ability they control”
  7. The loop's shape. Resolving ONE Exquisite Blood trigger gains you 1 life; that life gain triggers South Wind Avatar's second ability, which is put on the stack ON TOP of the remaining Exquisite Blood triggers and therefore resolves next. So the sequence alternates strictly: one Exquisite Blood resolution, then one drain, then one Exquisite Blood resolution, then one drain. Each cycle nets +3 unresolved Exquisite Blood triggers on the stack, and neither card says 'may', so no player may decline any of it.
    Exactly one drain resolves per cycle. Each drain removes 1 life per seat (4 across the pod) and each cycle gains you exactly 1 life -- not 4, because the other three Exquisite Blood triggers from that drain are still waiting under the newer objects.
    CR 603.3 — “The ability becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
    CR 117.5 — “Then triggered abilities are put on the stack”
  8. The loop is mandatory but it is NOT a draw, and the limiter has a name. A 'loop' in the rules is a state in which a set of actions could be repeated INDEFINITELY, and the draw rule applies only to a loop 'repeating a sequence of events with no way to stop'. Neither condition is met here: every iteration changes the game state irreversibly -- each drain lowers every opponent's life by 1, adjusting four life totals that never rise again -- so the sequence cannot be repeated indefinitely and there IS a way to stop. It terminates after a finite, computable number of iterations when a state-based action ends the game. 732.4's bare 'only mandatory actions' sentence therefore does not reach this sequence, both because it imports 104.4b by its own cross-reference and because 732.1b's definition of a loop is unsatisfied.
    No draw. The loop runs to a forced conclusion instead.
    CR 732.1b — “a set of actions could be repeated indefinitely”
    CR 104.4b — “repeating a sequence of events with no way to stop, the game is a draw”
    CR 732.4 — “If a loop contains only mandatory actions, the game is a draw.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life”
  9. Count the iterations. Opponents start at 40 and each drain removes exactly 1 from each seat, so drain #40 is the one that brings all four seats to 0 simultaneously. Drain #1 followed the death trigger's life gain; every later drain #k (k >= 2) is preceded by exactly one Exquisite Blood resolution, so 39 Exquisite Blood triggers resolve before drain #40.
    Your life at the moment drain #40 resolves: 40 + 1 (death trigger) + 39 (Exquisite Blood resolutions) = 80. Per opponent: 40 - 40 = 0. Pod total: 160 - 160 = 0. 160 life removed across the pod, 40 per seat -- the two figures are not interchangeable.
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life”
  10. Drain #40 finishes resolving and a player would receive priority. State-based actions are checked FIRST, before the four new Exquisite Blood triggers from that drain are put on the stack (704.3, the same ordering cited at step 2). All four opponents have 0 life, so all four lose the game as a single simultaneous state-based action; because state-based actions were performed, the check repeats, and the waiting triggers never get put on the stack.
    Opponents 1-4 each at 0 life and each loses the game. Those four pending Exquisite Blood triggers never reach the stack, so your life stops at 80.
    CR 704.3 — “Whenever a player would get priority (see rule 117, “Timing and Priority”), the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event.”
    CR 704.3 — “otherwise all triggered abilities that are waiting to be put on the stack are put on the stack”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
  11. Each opponent that lost the game leaves the game. You are the only player left, so you win immediately and the game ends immediately. Nothing still on the stack resolves.
    You win. Carrion Feeder's activated ability (pos 0) never resolves, so Carrion Feeder never gets its +1/+1 counter and stays a 1/1. The 117 Exquisite Blood triggers still on the stack never resolve. The opponents controlled no objects, so 800.4a removes nothing.
    CR 104.5 — “If a player loses the game, that player leaves the game.”
    CR 104.2a — “This happens immediately and overrides all effects that would preclude that player from winning the game.”
    CR 104.1 — “A game ends immediately when a player wins, when the game is a draw, or when the game is restarted.”
    CR 800.4a — “When a player leaves the game, all objects (see rule 109) owned by that player leave the game”
  12. Robustness note, and the one figure that is reading-dependent. Even if one insisted Exquisite Blood saw the drain as a single event and triggered only once, the alternation in step 7 is unchanged -- one Exquisite Blood resolution between successive drains -- so the drain count (40), the final per-seat total (0) and the pod total (0) are identical under every reading. YOUR final life is not: under the four-triggers reading and under a once-for-1 reading it is 80, but under a once-for-the-full-4-life-lost reading it would be 40 + 1 + 39x4 = 197. The kill does not depend on the reading; your life total does.
    Opponent figures and the win are reading-independent. Your final life is 80 on the reading resolved in step 6, and only that figure would move under an alternative reading.
    CR 603.2c — “An ability triggers only once each time its trigger event occurs. However, it can trigger repeatedly if one event contains multiple occurrences.”
    CR 700.1 — “Anything that happens in a game is an event. Multiple events may take place during the resolution of a spell or ability.”
After the line
Your life
80
Your battlefield
Exquisite Blood, South Wind Avatar (3/4, deathtouch), Carrion Feeder (1/1, no counters -- its activated ability never resolved), Swamp (tapped), Swamp (tapped)
Opponents
Opponent 1: 0, Opponent 2: 0, Opponent 3: 0, Opponent 4: 0

Case A-014 The undying pair: Butcher Ghoul + Geralf's Messenger under Yawgmoth — is the loop unbounded, and what does each opponent lose per cycle?
Rules version June 19, 2026 · status: cleared in 1 review cycle(s) · tap to open Verified
The question put to the record
Resolve this stack. Then: can the pilot repeat the loop — alternating Yawgmoth activations that sacrifice whichever undying creature is counter-free while stripping the other's +1/+1 counter — indefinitely, or is 'unbounded' the wrong word? State precisely what bounds the loop, and how much life each opponent loses per full two-activation cycle.
  1. Resolve the top object: Zulaport Cutthroat's triggered ability (Geralf's Messenger, a creature you control, died).
    Each of the three opponents loses 1 life (40 -> 39, 40 -> 39, 40 -> 39) and you gain 1 life (20 -> 21; the 1 life for Yawgmoth's activation cost was already paid, per the scenario's stated life totals). Pod total so far: 3 life.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
  2. Resolve the next object: Geralf's Messenger's undying trigger. Check its intervening 'if' clause on resolution.
    The condition — it had no +1/+1 counters on it when it died — was true at the trigger event and remains true on resolution, so the ability resolves: Geralf's Messenger returns from your graveyard to the battlefield under your control with a +1/+1 counter on it. Its 'enters tapped' replacement effect applies, so it enters tapped. Its enters-the-battlefield ability triggers: 'When this creature enters, target opponent loses 2 life.'
    CR 702.93a — “Undying is a triggered ability. “Undying” means “When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, if it had no +1/+1 counters on it, return it to the battlefield under its owner’s control with a +1/+1 counter on it.””
    CR 603.4 — “If the ability triggers, it checks the stated condition again as it resolves. If the condition isn’t true at that time, the ability is removed from the stack and does nothing.”
    CR 614.1d — “Continuous effects that read “[This permanent] enters . . .” or “[Objects] enter [the battlefield] . . .” are replacement effects.”
    CR 603.6a — “Enters-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent enters the battlefield.”
  3. Before any player receives priority, the Messenger's enters trigger is put on the stack — above Yawgmoth's still-pending activated ability. You choose a target: opponent 1.
    The stack is now (top to bottom): Messenger's ETB trigger targeting opponent 1; Yawgmoth's activated ability targeting Butcher Ghoul.
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority.”
    CR 117.5 — “Then triggered abilities are put on the stack (see rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities”).”
  4. Resolve Geralf's Messenger's enters-the-battlefield trigger.
    Opponent 1 loses 2 life (39 -> 37). Opponents now at 37 / 39 / 39.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
  5. Resolve Yawgmoth, Thran Physician's activated ability (bottom of the original stack). Target check first.
    Butcher Ghoul is still on the battlefield and is a legal target. A -1/-1 counter is put on it — it now has one +1/+1 counter and one -1/-1 counter — and you draw a card. The draw is mandatory ('and draw a card', no 'may'). Library ~55 -> ~54.
    CR 608.2b — “If the spell or ability specifies targets, it checks whether the targets are still legal.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
  6. State-based actions are checked before any player receives priority: counter annihilation on Butcher Ghoul.
    N=1: one +1/+1 counter and one -1/-1 counter are removed from Butcher Ghoul. It is a 1/1 with NO counters — exactly the state undying needs for its next death. No player is at 0 or less life; no creature has lethal damage or 0 toughness. The stack is empty. Board: Yawgmoth, Butcher Ghoul (1/1, counter-free), Zulaport Cutthroat, Geralf's Messenger (tapped, one +1/+1 counter). You 21; opponents 37/39/39.
    CR 704.5q — “If a permanent has both a +1/+1 counter and a -1/-1 counter on it, N +1/+1 and N -1/-1 counters are removed from it, where N is the smaller of the number of +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters on it.”
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed.”
  7. LOOP ANALYSIS — the mirrored second activation. Pay 1 life, sacrifice Butcher Ghoul (now counter-free), targeting Geralf's Messenger with the -1/-1 counter.
    Butcher Ghoul died with no +1/+1 counters, so undying triggers and returns it with a +1/+1 counter; Zulaport Cutthroat triggers again (each opponent loses 1, you gain 1); the -1/-1 counter annihilates the Messenger's +1/+1 counter via the same state-based action, leaving the Messenger counter-free for the next Messenger-sacrifice; you draw a second card. The counter-stripping is what keeps the loop alive: a creature that dies WITH a +1/+1 counter fails undying's intervening 'if' and never triggers at all. Butcher Ghoul has no enters trigger, so the Ghoul half of the cycle drains only via Zulaport. PER FULL TWO-ACTIVATION CYCLE: each opponent loses 2 (Zulaport, one per death); the opponent targeted by the Messenger's enters trigger loses 2 more (so 4 total for that seat); pod total 8 (6 Zulaport + 2 Messenger). You pay 2 life and gain 2 from Zulaport — net 0 — and draw 2 cards.
    CR 702.93a — ““Undying” means “When this permanent is put into a graveyard from the battlefield, if it had no +1/+1 counters on it, return it to the battlefield under its owner’s control with a +1/+1 counter on it.””
    CR 603.4 — “When the trigger event occurs, the ability checks whether the stated condition is true. The ability triggers only if it is; otherwise it does nothing.”
    CR 704.5q — “If a permanent has both a +1/+1 counter and a -1/-1 counter on it, N +1/+1 and N -1/-1 counters are removed from it, where N is the smaller of the number of +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters on it.”
  8. LOOP BOUND — verify or refute 'unbounded'.
    'Unbounded' is REFUTED. Yawgmoth's draw is not optional: the resolving ability's instructions must be followed, and 'and draw a card' contains no 'may'. Every activation therefore consumes one library card. With ~55 cards in the library, the pilot can make at most ~55 activations (including the one just resolved) — about 27 full two-activation cycles — before the library is empty; the next mandatory draw after that would be from an empty library, and the pilot loses the game as a state-based action. The loop is BOUNDED BY LIBRARY SIZE, not by life (net 0 per cycle) or by bodies (the two undying creatures recycle indefinitely in principle). It is also not a rules-mandated loop: each iteration begins with a discretionary activation, so the pilot simply stops before the last safe draw — but a set of actions that cannot be 'repeated indefinitely' is, by the CR's own definition of a loop, not unbounded.
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 121.4 — “A player who attempts to draw a card from a library with no cards in it loses the game the next time a player would receive priority. (This is a state-based action. See rule 704.)”
    CR 704.5b — “If a player attempted to draw a card from a library with no cards in it since the last time state-based actions were checked, that player loses the game.”
    CR 732.1b — “Occasionally the game gets into a state in which a set of actions could be repeated indefinitely (thus creating a “loop”).”
  9. LETHALITY WITHIN THE BOUND — does KILL F still kill?
    Yes, comfortably. Over n activations each opponent loses n life to Zulaport (one death per activation) plus 2 per Messenger enters-trigger aimed at them; the Messenger trigger fires on every odd-numbered activation (ceil(n/2) times). At n = 30 activations (15 cycles), Zulaport alone deals 30 to each seat and the 15 Messenger triggers, spread 5/5/5, add 10 more — 40 per opponent, exactly lethal from 40, with each opponent losing the game as a state-based action at 0 or less life. 30 activations cost 30 draws against a ~55-card library, leaving ~25 cards of margin. Correct label for KILL F: not 'unbounded' but 'library-bounded (~55 iterations available), lethal to the pod in ~30'.
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”

Judge's DeskMANA MAP · VOL. 008

Featured Artist

by "Ledger" Lin Marginal
◆ DATA-DERIVED★ COACHING
The hands that painted your deck — counted and credited.
THE PAINT

SIXTY-EIGHT HANDS, NO HEADLINER

Eighty cards, sixty-eight signatures, and the deepest credit in the book is three. Nobody owns this deck's look — which is itself the story, and it was counted, not felt.

Start with the number that decides this section. Eighty cards counted, sixty-eight different signatures, and no artist painted more than three. There is no standout in this deck, so nobody is going to be told they are one.

Daniel Ljunggren holds the deepest credit at exactly three, and the trio is worth stopping on: Black Sun's Zenith, Jet Medallion, Skullclamp. All three are the deck's rare noncreature tools — the only sweeper whose counters the Nest can read, the discount that plays every black turn one spell deeper, and the one-mana engine that turns each 1/1 into two cards. One hand, three cards, and not a creature among them in a list that runs thirty.

One figure needs saying out loud, because it is the one that lies. Jorge Jacinto's Swamp appears 21 times here and counts once. Authorship is per card, never per copy — count it the other way and a basic land wins this section every single issue. Copies across the whole deck: 100.

The printings cluster where recent Commander cardboard comes from: eleven cards from Lorwyn Eclipsed Commander, ten from Secrets of Strixhaven Commander, seven from Tarkir: Dragonstorm Commander, five from Commander Masters. The analysis reports no contiguous collector-number run anywhere, so nothing here landed as an unopened product — this is breadth by accumulation, one card at a time, and it is most of why no single hand gets ahead.

Nothing is foil and nothing is borderless. Nine legendary frames, three enchantment frames, one spree. This is a working deck, sleeved at working prices, and it looks like one on purpose.
